| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-139 | P2 | ProviderBranch has a parent ProviderProfile reference, bilingual names, required location and doctor roster, but no business uniqueness for a branch, coordinate bounds/CRS/geocoding provenance, tenant/provider authorization, roster role/duplicate/active validation, branch lifecycle or version/CAS. Stale branches/doctors may remain selectable for discovery or booking. | `src/schemas/provider-branch.schema.ts:7–33`; `audit-artifacts/phase0b-backend/semantic-evidence-provider-branch-schema.md` | Bind branch to canonical provider/tenant, validate and normalize geo/name fields, enforce business uniqueness and roster membership, add active/closed/versioned lifecycle and audit controls, and test stale/concurrent updates and booking visibility. |


## Medical profile finding added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-140 | P1 | MedicalProfile binds uniquely to patient_id and documents authorized-provider visibility, but blood/gender/pregnancy/physiology fields lack runtime cross-field bounds, all chronic/allergy/surgery/medication/family arrays are `any[]`, and emergency contact/notes are unbounded sensitive text. The schema has no field-level provider/family access grants, consent/revocation, encryption/redaction, version/CAS, history or retention enforcement; last_updated_by_id is informational only. | `src/schemas/medical-profile.schema.ts:10–56`; `audit-artifacts/phase0b-backend/semantic-evidence-medical-profile-schema.md` | Add strict nested health DTOs and cross-field validation, canonical patient/family/provider authorization and consent scopes, sensitive-field protection/redaction/retention, versioned updates and durable audit/history; test owner/authorized-provider/stranger/unauth and stale-update cases. |


## Doctor profile extended finding added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-141 | P1 | DoctorProfileExtended binds one record to a doctor and stores institution refs, clinic/online/home prices, radius, insurance networks, gallery and schedule, but provider/hospital refs lack role/type/tenant validation; prices default to zero without currency/precision/bounds/effective version; schedule is arbitrary JSON; insurance/gallery arrays are unvalidated; and no verification/active state, approval actor, version/CAS, idempotency or audit history is present. | `src/modules/care/schemas/doctor-profile-extended.schema.ts:6–38`; `audit-artifacts/phase0b-backend/semantic-evidence-doctor-profile-extended-schema.md` | Add canonical provider/institution authorization, strict pricing/currency and schedule contracts, safe defaults, validated insurance/assets, verification and active lifecycle, versioned updates and durable audit/idempotency; test booking visibility and stale/concurrent updates. |


## Patient profile finding added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-142 | P1 | PatientProfile has indexed user binding plus health, emergency, address, identity and insurance fields, but user_id is not unique/canonically referenced; medical and identity arrays/values lack strict bounds and cross-field validation; notification/privacy/security settings are arbitrary objects; and sensitive national IDs, policy data, PDF URL, phone/email/DOB and emergency contacts lack field-level access, encryption/redaction, consent/revocation, retention, version/CAS or durable audit controls. | `src/schemas/patient-profile.schema.ts:5–81`; `audit-artifacts/phase0b-backend/semantic-evidence-patient-profile-schema.md` | Canonically bind one profile per user, add strict nested DTOs and health/insurance/address validation, protect settings and sensitive fields, enforce patient/provider/family access and consent, use versioned audited updates and retention/redaction, and test owner/authorized-provider/stranger/unauth cases. |

## Nabd extensions findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-143 | P0 | Wallet credit/debit reads a wallet, checks balance, saves the new balance, then creates a transaction in separate writes; no atomic conditional debit, amount validation, idempotency, unique-wallet invariant or transaction/session is enforced. | `src/modules/nabd-extensions/nabd-extensions.service.ts:125–164`; controller `:24–54` | Enforce finite positive amounts, authenticated role/ownership, unique wallet, atomic conditional balance update plus transaction ledger, idempotency/replay semantics and concurrency tests. |
| F-144 | P0 | Referral claim is a multi-write financial saga: non-atomic duplicate check/reward creation/useCount increment and two wallet credits; code uses `Math.random`; concurrent claims or partial failure can duplicate rewards or leave asymmetric balances. | `src/modules/nabd-extensions/nabd-extensions.service.ts:166–219` | Unique referral/reward constraints, cryptographically safe code generation, transaction/outbox or compensating saga, atomic use count and replay/concurrency/rollback tests. |
| F-145 | P1 | Patient timeline returns raw appointment, prescription, lab-result and vital documents, and health passport returns PHI plus a JWT/QR token without explicit scope, short TTL, audience/issuer, revocation, consent or verifier access controls. | `src/modules/nabd-extensions/nabd-extensions.service.ts:238–281`; controller `:85–93` | Define least-privilege DTO projections, consented patient/provider access, short-lived scoped passport artifact with revocation/audit, and PHI redaction tests. |
| F-146 | P1 | Care-program endpoint returns hard-coded Arabic programs, rewards, session text and a fixed `09:00` time; it only defines diabetes/pregnancy masters while accepting arbitrary program/session values, so product state and displayed schedule can be untruthful. | `src/modules/nabd-extensions/nabd-extensions.service.ts:283–371` | Source program catalog/schedule/rewards from governed data, validate enums/session membership, persist real completion state and test empty/unavailable states without fabricated values. |
| F-147 | P1 | Provider matching/ranking returns broad provider records and uses inconsistent location fields (`geo` versus ProviderProfile `location`); ranking substitutes constant rating/SLA/cancellation metrics and optional sponsorship, producing non-truthful ordering. | service `:377–433, 483–508`; `src/schemas/provider-profile.schema.ts:49–59, 128–132`; controller `:116–130` | Use canonical geospatial/indexed queries and real versioned metrics, validate coordinates/type, enforce public projection/PII redaction and ranking explainability tests. |
| F-148 | P0 | Nursing attendance/checklist and inventory flows lack service-layer ownership/provider/role checks; attendance uses a Riyadh default coordinate when booking location is absent, while checklist and inventory can be queried by identifier/account without scope. | service `:564–631`; controller `:142–161` | Reject missing location rather than fallback, authorize nurse/provider/patient relation and visit state, scope inventory to authenticated provider, validate coordinates and audit access. |
| F-149 | P1 | SLA cron and fraud detection are not concurrency-safe: SLA selects and updates orders without claim/lease or unique breach key; fraud uses check-then-create; broad catches silently suppress operational/collection errors. | service `:435–481, 510–558` | Add atomic claims/leases, unique event keys, observable error handling/retry/dead-letter policy and multi-worker duplicate/concurrency tests. |
| F-150 | P0 | Lab barcode verification and pharmacy broadcast/ad bid handlers return success after logging arbitrary request bodies without binding/verifying the referenced sample/broadcast/bid; they create false-success contracts. | controller `:152–171, 184–187` | Implement real persistence/state transitions, validate DTOs and ownership/role, return truthful status/errors, add replay and end-to-end persistence assertions. |
| F-151 | P0 | Corporate credit lookup uses `companyName` only and ignores `employeeId`; it increments usedCredit and saves without atomic reservation, idempotency or employee/company authorization, permitting cross-employee misuse and race overspend. | service `:688–701`; controller `:190–193` | Resolve canonical corporate/employee identity, validate amount, atomically reserve within limits with idempotency/audit and concurrency/authorization tests. |
| F-152 | P1 | Appointment repository imports Appointment from `schemas/extra.schemas` while the module registers AppointmentSchema from `schemas/appointment.schema`; VitalReading repository is typed as `any`, creating model/type drift and weakening compile-time contract guarantees. | `src/modules/nabd-extensions/repositories/appointment.repository.ts:1–13`; `src/modules/nabd-extensions/repositories/vitalreading.repository.ts:1–13`; module `:18–24,61–67` | Make repository imports/types match the registered canonical schemas and add module integration tests proving model token, collection and document shape alignment. |

## Notifications findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-153 | P1 | Admin notification send/schedule accepts `body:any`, arbitrary user/role targeting, raw title/body, params and action; only `scheduled_at` presence is checked, so the notification surface lacks typed audience/content allowlists and payload classification. | `src/modules/notifications/notifications.controller.ts:46–58`; `src/modules/notifications/notifications.service.ts:30–59`; `src/schemas/notification.schema.ts:9–24` | Add strict DTOs, permitted audience/content/action schemas, server-derived actor, approval/re-auth for sensitive broadcasts, idempotency and audit tests. |
| F-154 | P1 | Lifecycle event handlers create notifications without a durable event idempotency key/outbox or transition deduplication; repeated event delivery can duplicate patient/provider/admin notifications and deep links. | `src/modules/notifications/notifications.service.ts:316–852`; `src/schemas/notification.schema.ts:21–29` | Persist source event identity/version, unique dedup key and durable outbox/consumer retry semantics; test replay, ordering and partial failure. |
| F-155 | P1 | User/role/all listing exposes stored params/action/delivery metadata and role broadcasts through a broad `$or`; event params can carry PHI, insurance or finance amounts into push/SMS/WhatsApp/email without a visible sensitivity policy or projection/redaction boundary. | service `:280–295, 322–852`; schema `:9–24` | Define channel-specific least-privilege projections, audience isolation, PHI/financial redaction and retention/expiry; test patient/provider/admin cross-audience access. |
| F-156 | P1 | Delivery status can be reported as `SENT` when there are no channel values, while missing FCM credentials returns false and WhatsApp failures are swallowed; queue failure falls back to direct delivery without a durable attempt/lease model. | `src/modules/notifications/notifications.service.ts:57–119, 190–239, 262–278`; schema `:21–24` | Use explicit `UNDELIVERABLE/FAILED` semantics, provider receipts, bounded channel retry/dead-letter state and observable fallback; test each channel and all-disabled configuration. |
| F-157 | P1 | Notification payloads use raw title/body keys in email HTML and provider payloads, with no visible escaping/sanitization; arbitrary action routes/payloads are serialized into push deep links, creating content injection and route-confusion risk. | service `:159–165, 207–209, 246–254`; controller `:46–58` | Resolve trusted localized templates, escape HTML, allowlist deep-link routes/payload shapes, classify sensitive content and test malicious input. |
| F-158 | P1 | Notification controller defines `POST ':id/read'` before `POST 'read-all'`; depending on Nest route matching, literal `read-all` can be captured as an id route, and read operations have no idempotency/read-event audit beyond `read_by`. | `src/modules/notifications/notifications.controller.ts:36–44`; `src/modules/notifications/notifications.service.ts:298–314` | Make route matching unambiguous, add typed read contracts/idempotency and audit/read timestamp semantics, and test both paths and replay. |

## Health findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-159 | P0 | Health mutation idempotency is inconsistent: refill, refill snooze, chronic cancel, sleep add and emergency-contact add/remove lack `@RequireIdempotency`, while duplicate alias refill routes exist; retries can create duplicate orders/records or repeat state changes. | `src/modules/health/health.controller.ts:50–93, 111–123` | Apply one explicit idempotency policy to every mutation, remove/alias routes deliberately, persist replay responses and test retries/duplicates for each mutation. |
| F-160 | P0 | Refill is a multi-write order/tag/reminder saga. A reservation field prevents some concurrent creation but has no visible TTL/cleanup, and failure after order creation before reminder linkage can leave an orphan order or stuck lock. | `src/modules/health/health.service.ts:476–557`; `src/schemas/health.schema.ts:48–53` | Use durable transaction/outbox or compensating state machine, expiring lock/lease recovery, idempotency and failure-injection/concurrency tests; never claim fulfillment from order creation. |
| F-161 | P1 | SleepReading accepts raw score, duration, measured_at and source after only presence checks; schema has no bounds/enums/date validation, so impossible or misleading health-score inputs can persist. | service `:595–614`; `src/schemas/health.schema.ts:58–68`; controller `:86–93` | Add strict DTO/schema ranges and source/date policy, reject invalid data, and test device/manual provenance and boundary values. |
| F-162 | P1 | MedicationReminder stores `log:any[]` and appends via read/array rewrite; auto-created dispense reminders use fixed 08:00/20:00/tablet/7-day defaults and existence check, allowing lost updates/duplicate reminders and unverified schedule assumptions. | service `:356–381, 384–429`; schema `:32–45` | Type dose-log entries, add atomic append/dedup key and governed medication schedule source, preserve timezone/provenance and test concurrent/offline replay behavior. |
| F-163 | P1 | Emergency-contact primary updates are separate bulk update and push operations; add/remove are un-idempotent and lack uniqueness/count/version controls, so concurrent requests can produce multiple primaries or lost updates while storing sensitive contacts. | `src/modules/health/health.service.ts:687–730`; controller `:111–123`; `src/schemas/health.schema.ts:24–29` | Atomically enforce one primary, typed contact constraints, idempotency/CAS and audit/consent/retention rules with concurrency tests. |
| F-164 | P1 | Health score/trend logic embeds clinical thresholds and recommendations in application code and exposes reports/prescriptions from raw collections; the read shows no clinical governance/versioning/disclaimer or complete field-level projection for all sensitive fields. | service `:167–262, 619–685, 762–797`; controller `:95–109, 136–139` | Govern/version clinical rules, label decision-support limitations, enforce least-privilege clinical projections and test PHI minimization and truthful insufficient-data behavior. |

## Health dashboard finding added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-165 | P1 | Admin health dashboard returns broad infrastructure/config and operational telemetry, recent system-event metadata, queue names/depths and static cron claims; failures collapse to null/empty and no visible freshness, redaction, access audit or proof of actual cron registration is included. | `src/modules/health/health-dashboard.controller.ts:12–133` | Keep strict admin authorization, minimize/redact sensitive metadata, include freshness/source/unknown states, audit access, distinguish probe failure from zero/empty and derive cron/queue health from authoritative runtime state. |

## Push findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-166 | P0 | Push token registration upserts by token and rewrites its `user_id` from the current authenticated caller without token possession/attestation; a stolen or reassigned token can be rebound and receive another user’s notifications. | `src/modules/push/push.module.ts:28–43,354–373`; controller `:692–704` | Bind tokens through platform attestation/registration challenge, prevent unsafe reassignment, audit device ownership and test cross-user/token replay. |
| F-167 | P1 | Push delivery can return zero/no-op when no active tokens or configuration is missing, while the notification path can still create optimistic delivery records; channel failures and retries have no single durable idempotency/lease contract across PushService and NotificationsService. | `src/modules/push/push.module.ts:397–418,412–613`; `src/modules/notifications/notifications.service.ts:57–119` | Define explicit undeliverable/failed state, unified event/message id, durable attempt/lease/retry ledger and provider receipt tests. |
| F-168 | P1 | Client engagement endpoint accepts arbitrary data after only event enum validation, and booking/chat/call/payment/report listeners trust `any` payloads and push names, message bodies, call IDs and payment amounts without sensitivity/redaction or source-event deduplication. | `src/modules/push/push.module.ts:302–312,619–687`; controller `:732–734` | Validate event schemas, authenticate/authorize notification ownership, minimize/redact sensitive payloads, deduplicate source events and test malicious/replayed engagement. |
| F-169 | P1 | Push controller exposes raw device/web-subscription bodies and a test route with success-like wording; VAPID key route returns success with null key when unconfigured, and admin campaign route accepts raw input but only returns a blocked message. | `src/modules/push/push.module.ts:697–743` | Add strict DTOs/config truthfulness, explicit unavailable state, idempotent subscribe/unsubscribe and remove misleading fake-success/test production surfaces. |

## Users findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-170 | P1 | Privacy and security settings use generic `setSetting` with arbitrary objects, while profile editing still permits broad sensitive fields such as phone/email/national_id/notes; the controller accepts raw bodies and only notification settings have strict normalization/idempotency. | `src/modules/users/users.service.ts:138–169,273–283`; controller `:66–88` | Define per-setting DTOs/allowlists, re-auth for sensitive changes, audit/version/CAS and idempotent owner tests; minimize and protect PII. |
| F-171 | P1 | Password change writes a new hash but does not visibly rotate/revoke existing refresh sessions or enforce idempotency/re-auth policy; session-list Redis failures are returned as an empty list, conflating unavailable with no sessions. | service `:286–331`; controller `:86–99` | Require current-session assurance, rotate/revoke affected tokens, persist audit, make retries safe and expose Redis unavailable distinctly. |
| F-172 | P1 | Address CRUD spreads raw request fields, uses full-array read/modify/write for default/delete/update, returns `null` or success for missing records, and lacks idempotency, bounds, coordinate validation and concurrency control. | `src/modules/users/users.addresses.controller.ts:11–57` | Use typed address DTOs, atomic/CAS updates, explicit 404/409 behavior, one-default invariant, idempotency and owner/stranger/replay tests. |
| F-173 | P1 | Canonical insurance POST merges raw body and returns it with `verified:false`, but `UsersService.updatePatientProfile` editable whitelist does not include `insurance`; the route can therefore report a successful update without persisting the canonical insurance object. | `src/modules/users/users.insurance.controller.ts:56–80`; `src/modules/users/users.service.ts:138–157` | Add explicit server-controlled insurance write path/DTO, validate and protect sensitive fields, verify persistence/reload and test verification lifecycle. |
| F-174 | P1 | `/users/me/insurance` and deprecated `/user/insurance` expose incompatible canonical `insurance` and legacy `insurance_policies[]` shapes, while `/insurance/active` is a third projection; migration/precedence and consistency are not enforced. | `src/modules/users/users.insurance.controller.ts:34–80`; `src/modules/users/user.insurance.controller.ts:13–46` | Establish one canonical source, migration/compatibility contract, least-privilege projection and cross-route consistency tests. |
| F-175 | P1 | Data-retention cron claims to delete associated PII after configurable days but only deletes User documents; PatientProfile/ProviderProfile/media/medical/insurance/session and related records are not handled here, and failures are only logged. | `src/modules/users/data-retention.service.ts:15–40` | Define lawful retention matrix and deletion workflow across collections/storage, dependency ordering, tombstone/audit evidence, retry/alerting and deletion verification tests. |
| F-176 | P1 | Wishlist toggle accepts an arbitrary itemId and adds an id-only record without resolving a canonical product or enforcing item existence/state; retry/concurrent toggles use read/modify/write and can yield misleading success. | `src/modules/users/users.service.ts:26–43`; controller `:39–47` | Validate canonical catalog identity/availability, use atomic set semantics/idempotency and test missing item, owner/replay/concurrency cases. |

## Auth schema findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-177 | P1 | Trusted-device records hash the raw token, which is good, but have no visible expiry/last-used cutoff, device attestation/fingerprint binding, revoke reason/history or active-device limit; retained enrollment/last IP and user-agent are sensitive security telemetry. | `src/modules/auth/schemas/trusted-device.schema.ts:7–28` | Add bounded TTL/absolute expiry, device binding/step-up verification, explicit revoke audit and retention policy; test token replay, revocation, expiry and cross-user isolation. |
| F-178 | P1 | Passkey credential persistence stores public key and counter but exposes no explicit revoked/deleted/attestation lifecycle fields; credential management and counter-update race behavior cannot be proven from this schema and must be enforced by consumers. | `src/modules/auth/schemas/passkey-credential.schema.ts:4–35` | Define credential lifecycle/attestation policy, atomic signature-counter advancement and audit; test assertion replay, cloned authenticator and revoked credential behavior. |
| F-179 | P1 | Password validator requires eight characters, uppercase, digit and special character but lacks maximum length, breach/common-password screening, normalization/history policy, and its application across all create/reset/change DTOs is not established by the decorator alone. | `src/common/validators/password.validator.ts:3–27` | Apply one documented password policy to every credential flow, bound input cost/length, screen breached/common values and test all auth paths. |

## Auth execution findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-180 | P0 | Refresh-session persistence is best-effort: if Redis is unavailable, login can still issue refresh tokens without server-side rotation/replay tracking; token refresh is accepted in a public request body rather than cookie-only contract. | `src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts:46–70,73–105`; `src/modules/auth/auth.controller.ts:241–247` | Fail closed or use a durable session store, enforce cookie/CSRF contract, atomically rotate/revoke families and test Redis failure/replay/device mismatch. |
| F-181 | P1 | Consent is appended to a mutable `User.legal_consents` array with no dedicated immutable compliance record, policy allowlist, duplicate/version/CAS policy or proof of document text/version at acceptance. | `src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts:125–131`; controller `:255–263` | Persist immutable consent evidence with actor/time/policy/version/hash, typed allowlist and audit/replay tests. |
| F-182 | P0 | Patient registration creates User and PatientProfile in separate writes; failure can leave orphan accounts/profiles, and no idempotency/unique transaction contract is shown. | `src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts:349–405` | Use transaction/outbox/compensation plus unique identifiers and idempotent registration tests under failure/concurrency. |
| F-183 | P1 | Guest creation trusts client `deviceId`, creates fallback phone identities using time/random values, and guest conversion/merge performs best-effort multi-collection rewrites with swallowed errors before deleting the guest; partial merge/data loss and account confusion are possible. | `src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts:629–750`; controller `:169–179` | Bind device identity securely, make merge a resumable transactional state machine with idempotency/locks, verify every collection and test retries/partial failures. |
| F-184 | P0 | Apple, X and Snapchat token verification locally decodes JWT-like strings without signature/key/issuer/audience validation; X/Snapchat synthesize fallback emails for malformed tokens, enabling account takeover or arbitrary identity creation. | `src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts:912–1028` | Use official SDK/OIDC signature and claim validation for every provider, reject malformed/unverified tokens and test forged tokens, nonce/replay and account-linking. |
| F-185 | P1 | Legacy auth routes return bearer/session tokens in JSON (`register`, login/2FA/social/guest/refresh) while the patient exchange route uses httpOnly cookies; this creates inconsistent browser token exposure and contract ambiguity. | `src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts:407–447,449–515,517–545,634–667,912–960`; controller `:143–166,169–203,241–247,301–305` | Define one browser-safe cookie/session contract, remove token-in-body for web, bind refresh/CSRF/device semantics and test all clients. |
| F-186 | P1 | Passkey assertion counter update is read/verify/write without CAS/version enforcement, and verification diagnostics log RP/origin/credential owner details; enrollment/device removal mutations lack explicit idempotency. | `src/modules/auth/passkey.service.ts:117–150,175–205`; controller `:19–40` | Atomically advance counters, redact security logs, enforce credential lifecycle/idempotency and test concurrent/replayed assertions. |
| F-187 | P1 | Trusted-device validation has no absolute expiry and revoke returns success even when no matching device exists; heartbeat/online sessions store IP/UA and use `unknown-device` fallback, making online state and revocation semantics potentially misleading. | `src/modules/auth/device-trust.service.ts:45–115`; controller `:210–239` | Enforce expiry/active limits, return explicit 404/409, bind heartbeat to validated device/session, minimize retention and test expiry/revocation/unknown-device cases. |

## Support findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-188 | P1 | Support creation/reply accepts raw bodies and stores arbitrary attachments and `thread:any[]` plus user name/phone; no content/attachment type-size/URL policy, redaction, idempotency or concurrent append control is visible. | `src/modules/support/support.service.ts:17–54`; `src/schemas/support.schema.ts:21–38`; controller `:11–16` | Use strict DTOs, safe media references, bounds/content scanning, PII minimization, atomic append/idempotency and owner/stranger/replay tests. |
| F-189 | P1 | `/support/requests` and `/support/tickets` are duplicate create contracts, while `listTickets` queries `patient_id` although create stores `user_id`; the second list can silently diverge or return empty data. | `src/modules/support/support.controller.ts:11–16,23–26`; `src/modules/support/support.service.ts:18–36,73–76` | Choose one canonical route/field, provide migration compatibility deliberately and test create→list/detail across both contracts. |
| F-190 | P1 | Admin support status/assignment update mutates state with only enum validation; no assignment ownership, transition graph, idempotency, audit actor/reason or concurrency version is shown. | `src/modules/support/support.service.ts:57–70`; controller `:18–20`; schema `:32–37` | Enforce typed assignment/transition policy, CAS/idempotency, immutable admin audit and unauthorized/wrong-role/replay tests. |
| F-191 | P1 | Feedback endpoint returns `{success:true}` without persisting, validating or dispatching the feedback, creating a false-success contract and loss of user input. | `src/modules/support/support.service.ts:99–102`; controller `:34–36` | Persist or explicitly reject/unavailable, validate content and return truthful state with trace id; test failure and replay. |
| F-192 | P1 | FAQ content is hard-coded and settings schema stores unbounded strings/client push token without enum/type constraints; support schema lacks retention/classification controls for ticket PII and thread history. | `src/modules/support/support.service.ts:91–97`; `src/schemas/support.schema.ts:43–56` | Source FAQ/settings from governed contracts, validate values, secure push token handling and define PII/thread retention/redaction policy. |

## Webhooks findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-193 | P1 | Moyasar/PayTabs/SMS verification is fail-closed only in production; missing secrets are accepted in non-production, so a misclassified or accidentally exposed non-production endpoint can emit trusted payment/SMS events without authentication. | `src/modules/webhooks/webhooks.service.ts:30–63,107–117`; controller `:8–12` | Fail closed by default in every deployed environment, isolate local test adapters, and test missing-secret/misclassified-environment rejection. |
| F-194 | P0 | Webhook controllers accept arbitrary raw bodies/event names and emit them to the global event bus with no DTO, event allowlist, size limit or payload schema; forged/oversized/unexpected events can reach downstream payment or operational handlers if signature scope is bypassed or misconfigured. | service `:77–105,107–135`; controller `:14–49` | Validate provider-specific envelope/event allowlists and bounded schemas before emission, quarantine unknown events and test malicious/oversized payloads. |
| F-195 | P1 | Moyasar/PayTabs/LiveKit signatures use `rawBody || JSON.stringify(parsedBody)` fallback; if raw-body middleware is absent or altered, signature verification can diverge from provider bytes and create ambiguous acceptance behavior. | `src/modules/webhooks/webhooks.controller.ts:14–48`; service `:36–63,77–96,119–134` | Require verified raw bytes, reject missing raw body for signed providers, and test whitespace/order/encoding/signature cases end-to-end. |
| F-196 | P1 | Replay deduplication depends on Redis `SETNX` plus 24-hour TTL; Redis errors are not converted to a fail-closed/quarantine state, and deduplicated events return HTTP success without durable processing/audit evidence. | `src/modules/webhooks/webhooks.service.ts:65–105` | Use durable event inbox/idempotency ledger with provider event identity, explicit Redis-unavailable handling, processing outcome and replay tests. |
| F-197 | P1 | Webhook handlers are emit-only and do not persist verified provider event envelopes/status transitions in this boundary; downstream delivery/processing failure cannot be reconciled from a durable source of truth. | `src/modules/webhooks/webhooks.service.ts:77–135`; `src/modules/webhooks/webhooks.module.ts:5–10` | Persist immutable verified envelopes, processing state/errors and retry/DLQ linkage before/with emission; test crash/retry/reconciliation. |

## Community findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-198 | P0 | Guarded Community mutations substitute a literal shared `guest` identity whenever `req.user?.id` is absent, allowing unauthenticated/misconfigured requests to create or mutate content under one pseudo-user. | `src/modules/community/community.controller.ts:26–29,36–49,70–78`; controller `:9–10` | Reject missing authenticated identity with 401; remove shared fallback and test unauthenticated, cross-user and owner behavior. |
| F-199 | P1 | Admin pending/moderation routes are under the global JWT guard but show no explicit role restriction; service methods also accept no actor/role and can be invoked by any authenticated caller if guard does not add role policy. | `src/modules/community/community.controller.ts:51–61`; `community.service.ts:140–156` | Enforce admin/moderator roles and actor audit at route/service boundary; test patient/provider/admin authorization. |
| F-200 | P1 | Posts/comments/sessions accept raw or unbounded content and URLs, with free-form category/status/tag fields; no size, URL, HTML/script, enum, or retention controls are visible. | `src/modules/community/community.controller.ts:26–43,70–85`; `src/schemas/community.schemas.ts:5–65` | Use typed bounded DTOs, safe URL/media policy, content sanitization and retention; test XSS/oversize/invalid enum inputs. |
| F-201 | P1 | Keyword matching is the main automatic moderation gate and stores flagged comments as `removed` without review reason/actor/audit; misspellings, multilingual content and new safety classes are not governed. | `src/modules/community/community.service.ts:14–23,48–58,72–83`; schema `:31–45` | Implement governed moderation policy with reason/evidence/review state and human escalation; test safety taxonomy and false-positive/negative paths. |
| F-202 | P1 | Vote toggles read voter arrays and counters before separate updates; concurrent/replayed requests can produce counter/array drift and no idempotency ledger is present. | `src/modules/community/community.service.ts:104–130`; schema `:15–19` | Use atomic per-user vote state or transaction/CAS with reconciliation and replay/concurrency tests. |
| F-203 | P1 | Live-session join uses `$addToSet` and unconditional `$inc`, so repeated/concurrent joins can inflate `attendee_count`; session status mutation has no visible host/admin authorization or transition validation. | `src/modules/community/community.service.ts:180–197`; controller `:75–85`; schema `:47–63` | Atomically increment only on newly added attendee, enforce host/moderator transitions and idempotent join/status tests. |
| F-204 | P1 | Community mutation success does not include idempotency/event audit, while notification failures are swallowed and delete/moderation/session status have no durable actor/reason history; moderation and safety outcomes are not reconstructable. | `src/modules/community/community.service.ts:86–101,132–156,192–197`; module `:10–22` | Add immutable moderation/activity audit and durable event/outbox semantics, explicit failure handling and replay/reconciliation tests. |

## Medical Profile findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-205 | P0 | Medical Profile stores chronic diseases, allergies, surgeries, medications and family history as unvalidated `any[]`, while updates accept raw body fields; arbitrary PHI shape/content can be persisted and later exposed to clinical consumers. | `src/schemas/medical-profile.schema.ts:31–50`; `src/modules/medical-profile/medical-profile.service.ts:23–29,40–47`; controller `:20–32` | Define embedded DTO/schemas with strict bounds, enum/format validation, field-level authorization, encryption/minimization and PHI access tests. |
| F-206 | P1 | `BloodType` exists but `blood_type` is stored as free string; gender and numeric physiology/pregnancy fields lack enum/range/cross-field validation, allowing clinically incoherent records. | `src/schemas/medical-profile.schema.ts:5–29`; service `:23–29` | Enforce enum/ranges and pregnancy/breastfeeding consistency with typed validation and invalid-data tests. |
| F-207 | P1 | PHI list add/remove mutations use read/modify/write, accept arbitrary item bodies/list strings, return success with unchanged profile for missing item and have no idempotency or optimistic concurrency. | `src/modules/medical-profile/medical-profile.service.ts:40–57`; controller `:22–32` | Use allowlisted list operations, atomic/CAS updates, explicit 404/409 semantics and replay/concurrency tests. |
| F-208 | P1 | Health-passport endpoint issues a five-minute JWT QR token with scope/type but no visible audience/recipient binding, issuance audit, revocation or one-time use state; medical-profile schema has no passport state. | `src/modules/medical-profile/medical-profile.controller.ts:14–18`; `src/schemas/medical-profile.schema.ts:52–56` | Bind token to intended recipient/purpose/audience, persist/audit issuance and use, support revocation and test QR replay/cross-context disclosure. |
| F-209 | P1 | Provider medical-profile access is hard-blocked, which avoids current BOLA but leaves the documented provider-read clinical workflow unavailable; no consent/active-care relationship contract is implemented in this surface. | `src/modules/medical-profile/medical-profile.service.ts:31–38`; controller `:34–36` | Implement only through explicit consent/relationship/least-privilege contract with audit and owner/stranger/consent tests; otherwise keep endpoint clearly unavailable. |
| F-210 | P1 | PHI schema has only last_updated_at/by audit fields and no consent, provenance, version, retention or encryption metadata; clinical changes and deletion obligations cannot be reconstructed from this model. | `src/schemas/medical-profile.schema.ts:46–54` | Add immutable change/audit trail, provenance/version and retention/deletion policy with access-log and lifecycle verification. |

## Media findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-211 | P1 | Upload validation trusts client filename extension and MIME; no magic-byte/content scan, antivirus, decompression-bomb, filename normalization or purpose-specific type policy is visible. | `src/modules/media/media.controller.ts:19–40,53–65`; `src/modules/media/media.service.ts:39–51` | Validate bytes server-side, enforce purpose/type/size policy, scan/quarantine before exposure and test spoofed/oversized/malicious files. |
| F-212 | P1 | Presigned upload creates a MediaAsset before object completion and has no completion/ETag/hash/scan state; abandoned or untrusted objects can remain as valid asset records. | `src/modules/media/media.controller.ts:53–71`; `src/modules/media/media.schema.ts:8–18` | Use pending→uploaded→scanned state, completion callback/check, content hash and orphan cleanup/reconciliation. |
| F-213 | P1 | Signed download checks owner or chat participant, but chat authorization is embedded query logic over ChatThread/FamilyGroup/Appointment and depends on participant arrays/booking status; no durable access audit or explicit clinical consent proof is shown. | `src/modules/media/media.controller.ts:73–155` | Centralize relationship/consent authorization, minimize projections, audit every PHI download and test cross-thread/expired-booking/family cases. |
| F-214 | P1 | Admin delete accepts an arbitrary storage key and deletes the object without resolving/deleting the MediaAsset record; stale metadata and accidental/wrong-bucket deletion remain possible despite role restriction. | `src/modules/media/media.controller.ts:158–173`; `src/modules/media/media.service.ts:84–97` | Delete by asset ID with ownership/record linkage, audit actor/reason, idempotent 404 semantics and storage/DB reconciliation. |
| F-215 | P1 | Upload/presigned/delete operations lack visible idempotency and retry contracts; compensation on buffer-upload DB failure is best-effort and can leave orphaned storage objects. | `src/modules/media/media.controller.ts:39–50,65–70,163–171` | Add idempotency and durable cleanup/outbox, explicit retry state and failure/replay tests. |
| F-216 | P1 | MediaAsset stores original_name, mime_type and size_bytes without bounds, content hash, retention/deletion state or scan provenance; medical/report/chat files cannot be governed through this schema alone. | `src/modules/media/media.schema.ts:8–23` | Add bounded metadata, hash/scan provenance, classification, retention/legal hold and verified deletion lifecycle. |

## SEO findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-217 | P1 | Slug resolution constructs an unescaped regular expression from user-controlled slug text for fuzzy name lookup; free-form type/slug inputs can trigger unintended matches or expensive queries. | `src/modules/seo/seo.service.ts:60–81` | Validate type against enum, escape/bound regex or use normalized indexed equality, cap input/latency and test regex metacharacters/large inputs. |
| F-218 | P1 | `meta()` returns the full resolved entity under `entity`, so public SEO metadata can expose fields beyond the intended crawl/share projection, including sensitive or operational catalogue attributes. | `src/modules/seo/seo.service.ts:83–125`; controller `:26–31` | Return a strict public projection per entity type, separate internal DTOs and test hidden-field absence. |
| F-219 | P1 | Sitemap XML interpolates slugs/lastmod values without explicit XML escaping and silently caps each entity type at 5,000 rows; valid public pages can be malformed or undiscoverable. | `src/modules/seo/seo.service.ts:221–258` | Escape XML values, paginate/index sitemap sets with sitemap index, monitor count/health and test special characters/cap overflow. |
| F-220 | P1 | robots.txt broadly allows `/` and only disallows selected paths while SEO routes expose public resolution/meta/sitemap; the crawl policy is not proven against all private/PII routes and cache invalidation/lifecycle. | `src/modules/seo/seo.service.ts:264–281`; controller `:12–76` | Maintain route-derived robots policy, verify private exclusions/404/410 and test crawler access across every sensitive route. |
| F-221 | P1 | llms.txt contains hard-coded coverage, payment and service claims and swallows catalogue query failures, so AI crawlers may receive incomplete or inaccurate statements not grounded in current published data. | `src/modules/seo/seo.service.ts:289–397` | Generate only verified live claims with provenance/timestamps, fail/report incomplete sections and test stale/empty/failure datasets. |
| F-222 | P1 | IndexNow notification is fire-and-forget and all network failures are swallowed; no durable queue, retry, dedup, deletion/410 lifecycle or delivery observability is visible. | `src/modules/seo/seo.service.ts:399–433` | Persist lifecycle events, retry/DLQ with status and deletion handling, expose metrics and test failures/replay/new-update-delete flows. |

## AI findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-223 | P0 | Many AI routes accept authenticated requests without explicit patient-owner binding; only triage/skin visibly reject the shared guest identity, while OCR/voice/meal/plans/barcode/copilot can process or return clinical outputs without a demonstrated subject/access contract. | `src/modules/ai/ai.controller.ts:65–168`; `src/modules/ai/ai.service.ts:134–216,260–337` | Define per-feature subject/owner/role policy, reject missing identity where PHI/paid use applies, and test unauthenticated/stranger/owner access. |
| F-224 | P0 | AI inputs include raw transcripts, base64 images/audio and clinical notes with no centralized size/content/PHI redaction/data-retention policy; prompts are sent to external providers and provider fallback can change data jurisdiction. | `src/modules/ai/ai.service.ts:60–68,134–216,293–325`; `src/modules/ai/ai-provider.service.ts:193–231`; gateway `:193–218` | Add input limits/scanning/redaction, explicit consent/data-processing/provider residency policy, no-training guarantees and access/retention audit. |
| F-225 | P1 | Voice/OCR/medicine/barcode/diet/exercise failures return empty arrays, `Unknown` values or empty plans, which can be consumed as successful outputs and create false or unsafe downstream decisions. | `src/modules/ai/ai.service.ts:139–149,207–216,260–277,293–325` | Return explicit unavailable/error state with provenance/confidence and never treat fallback placeholders as domain facts; test provider/parser failure. |
| F-226 | P0 | `AiGatewayService` stores provider API keys in the `ai_providers` MongoDB collection and returns a patch containing the requested update; no encryption/key-vault/rotation/audit access control is visible. | `src/modules/ai/ai-gateway.service.ts:14–18,27–50,82–109,252–257` | Keep secrets in a managed secret store, redact all responses/logs, enforce dual-control/audit/rotation and test DB-read/admin compromise scenarios. |
| F-227 | P1 | Provider routing automatically falls back across configured vendors and DB-backed mode/pinning is cached; a request can cross provider/model boundaries after transient failure without a feature-specific consent, residency, safety or output-equivalence policy. | `src/modules/ai/ai-gateway.service.ts:129–176`; provider `:76–98,146–190` | Govern fallback chain per feature/data class, record selected provider and consent, fail closed where required and test fallback privacy/clinical consistency. |
| F-228 | P1 | Daily quota increment and usage logging are best-effort and not visibly atomically coupled to admission; failed usage writes/quota updates can permit over-quota use or make cost/audit reports incomplete. | `src/modules/ai/ai-gateway.service.ts:220–234`; provider `:114–143` | Use atomic quota reservation/usage ledger, durable eventing and reconciliation, with over-quota/concurrency/failure tests. |
| F-229 | P1 | Admin gateway mutation methods can update provider API keys/models/mode in DB, while their HTTP controller routes intentionally throw ServiceUnavailable; the implemented service surface and exposed contract are divergent and lack visible change audit. | `src/modules/ai/ai.controller.ts:26–52`; `src/modules/ai/ai-gateway.service.ts:236–267` | Define one authoritative admin contract, remove dead mutation paths or expose guarded audited routes, and test role/validation/replay/rollback. |

## Prescriptions findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-230 | P0 | Patient prescription upload accepts a client-supplied image and arbitrary item body, can create manual medicine entries, swallows creation failures, and records the result with `CREATED_BY_DOCTOR` despite patient origin; downstream actors can misread provenance/state. | `src/modules/prescriptions/prescriptions.service.ts:154–203`; controller `:17–20`; schema `:31–50` | Add typed upload/OCR contract, explicit patient-uploaded state/provenance, quarantine/review lifecycle and never convert failure into a usable clinical record. |
| F-231 | P1 | Prescription item medication/diagnosis/note/instruction fields are optional and unbounded; raw controller bodies and manual items permit malformed or unsafe clinical instructions. | `src/schemas/prescription.schema.ts:6–28,31–50`; controller `:11–42`; service `:77–128,154–191` | Enforce DTO/schema bounds, medication dose/frequency/quantity/date validation, sanitization and clinically safe review tests. |
| F-232 | P1 | Patient/manual upload may create a manual medicine entry or persist a line with no medicine ID; errors are swallowed and the line remains in the prescription, requiring downstream consumers to distinguish unresolved medication safely. | `src/modules/prescriptions/prescriptions.service.ts:163–191`; schema `:8–22` | Use explicit unresolved state/error reason, durable review queue and block dispensing/order use until approved substitute/verification. |
| F-233 | P1 | `sendToPharmacy` accepts client-supplied `pharmacy_id` and mutates prescription state separately from event emission; no pharmacy eligibility/assignment contract or idempotent/CAS transition is visible. | `src/modules/prescriptions/prescriptions.service.ts:230–242`; controller `:28–32` | Resolve pharmacy from governed server contract, atomically transition with outbox/idempotency and test owner/role/replay/race paths. |
| F-234 | P1 | Prescription transition/substitution saves state and emits events in separate operations without visible transaction/outbox; retries or crashes can leave state and notifications inconsistent. | `src/modules/prescriptions/prescriptions.service.ts:205–227,244–263` | Use atomic transition/event delivery, audit actor/reason and reconciliation with failure/replay tests. |
| F-235 | P1 | Prescription `id` is generated but not visibly unique/indexed in the schema, while all item-level and detail routes rely on it for lookup; collision/enumeration/performance guarantees are incomplete. | `src/schemas/prescription.schema.ts:31–38`; service `:205–207,230–232,322–329` | Add unique/indexed server identifier and collision/backfill verification; keep foreign lookups 404. |
| F-236 | P1 | Prescription schema lacks provenance, consent/access audit, version, retention/deletion and immutable review history despite containing PHI and medication decisions; list/read APIs can expose complete records to participants without field-level minimization beyond the patient detail helper. | `src/schemas/prescription.schema.ts:31–50`; service `:276–291,322–329` | Add immutable PHI access/review history, provenance/version/retention controls and strict DTO projections per role. |

## Billing findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-237 | P0 | Invoice issue is check-then-create without unique compound booking identity or transaction/idempotency; concurrent GET/PDF/email calls can create duplicate invoices and consume multiple invoice numbers. | `src/modules/billing/billing.module.ts:68–77,90–120`; controller `:214–232` | Enforce unique `(booking_kind, booking_id, status/lifecycle)` policy, atomic upsert/idempotency and duplicate/replay/concurrency tests. |
| F-238 | P0 | Invoice total is derived from whichever booking field is present (`total`, `total_price` or `price`) and is trusted as payable truth without a cross-check against settled payment/ledger; a stale or manipulated booking total can produce a false invoice. | `src/modules/billing/billing.module.ts:90–103` | Source amount from authoritative paid ledger/payment intent, reconcile currency/line items and reject unverified settlement. |
| F-239 | P1 | The helper implements only simplified five-tag TLV QR while comments explicitly defer Fatoora clearance, UBL XML and cryptographic signing; an invoice may be labeled tax-compliant beyond the implemented phase. | `src/modules/billing/billing.module.ts:2–6,45–59,151–184` | Label phase accurately and complete certified ZATCA requirements before claiming full compliance; validate QR tags/encoding against official acceptance tests. |
| F-240 | P1 | `booking_kind` is free-form at route/service boundary and maps to models; invoice fields/totals/VAT/currency/status lack strict enum/range/cross-field validation and no visible booking-kind-specific projection prevents inconsistent source values. | `src/modules/billing/billing.module.ts:79–87,27–43`; controller `:215–245` | Use allowlisted kind DTOs, strict money/currency/VAT validation and per-kind server-owned amount/line-item contracts. |
| F-241 | P1 | Invoice ownership accepts only `booking.patient_id === user.id || booking.user_id === user.id` or literal lowercase `user.role === 'admin'`, diverging from effective-role conventions and risking false denials or inconsistent privileged access. | `src/modules/billing/billing.module.ts:91–99`; controller `:240–245` | Centralize effective-role/ownership policy, return 404 for foreign records and test patient/admin/provider/stranger/role variants. |
| F-242 | P1 | PDF and email include booking identifiers, optional patient name, item names/prices and HTML-derived invoice values without visible role-specific minimization, HTML escaping, access audit or durable delivery status. | `src/modules/billing/billing.module.ts:131–205` | Use escaped templates and minimized DTOs, audit PDF/email access, record delivery/retry state and test PII injection/cross-user retrieval. |
| F-243 | P1 | EInvoice has ISSUED/CANCELLED fields but no visible cancellation endpoint, immutable invoice version/provenance, payment/ledger reconciliation, retention or deletion/legal-hold policy. | `src/modules/billing/billing.module.ts:27–43,90–120,123–129` | Implement immutable fiscal lifecycle, cancellation/credit-note rules, ledger reconciliation and retention/legal-hold controls. |

## Timeline findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-244 | P1 | Timeline fans out one query per requested kind, each with its own limit, then loads reminders and expands log entries before final truncation; free-form kinds/limit can amplify database work and produce unstable partial feeds. | `src/modules/timeline/timeline.service.ts:105–121,321–366`; controller `:17–31` | Allowlist kinds, bound limit/date windows, use bounded per-source pagination/aggregation and performance/load tests. |
| F-245 | P1 | Unified events expose raw source-derived titles, diagnoses, order totals, symptoms, summaries and links across many PHI/financial models; only the final shape is typed, not the content projection per role/sensitivity. | `src/modules/timeline/timeline.service.ts:118–155,159–198,242–297,301–359` | Define per-kind minimized patient DTOs, redact sensitive fields, audit access and test hidden PHI/financial attributes. |
| F-246 | P1 | Controller accepts arbitrary date strings and limits; invalid dates can enter Mongo range queries and negative/huge limits can alter query behavior, with no explicit validation error contract. | `src/modules/timeline/timeline.controller.ts:17–31`; service `:105–114` | Validate ISO dates/order/range and bounded positive limits with typed DTOs and invalid-input tests. |
| F-247 | P1 | Missing/invalid identity produces an empty feed/summary instead of an explicit authentication failure, which can hide auth regressions and makes absence indistinguishable from a real empty patient record. | `src/modules/timeline/timeline.service.ts:105–110,369–372`; controller `:17–39` | Enforce authenticated identity at guard/service boundary and test unauthenticated vs authenticated-empty semantics. |
| F-248 | P1 | Static status map defaults unknown states to `active` and omits several domain states, so newly introduced or unmapped source states can be displayed as falsely active. | `src/modules/timeline/timeline.service.ts:32–78,123–133,264–274` | Version/status-contract map with explicit `unknown` state, monitoring and fixture tests for every source enum. |
| F-249 | P1 | Timeline feed and summary run many independent reads without snapshot/version consistency; concurrent state changes can make counts, event ordering and source totals disagree. | `src/modules/timeline/timeline.service.ts:118–366,369–385` | Use consistent read strategy/materialized event contract or documented eventual consistency with reconciliation tests. |
| F-250 | P1 | Timeline appointment repository imports `Appointment` from `extra.schemas` while module registers `AppointmentSchema` from `appointment.schema`; all repositories are generic wrappers with no projection/ownership layer, so model resolution and PHI filtering depend on wiring correctness. | `src/modules/timeline/timeline.module.ts:5–25,27–44`; `src/modules/timeline/repositories/appointment.repository.ts:1–13` and peer wrappers | Collapse to canonical model/schema imports, verify runtime model identity and add integration tests for patient isolation and projection. |

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## Referral findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-258 | P1 | Referral service persists codes/invites in `users.referral_code` and `referral_invites`, while the declared schemas define separate `referral_codes` and `referral_rewards` collections; uniqueness, lifecycle and reward records are therefore split from the documented model. | `src/modules/referral/referral.service.ts:32–34,47–55,115–128`; `src/schemas/referral.schema.ts:7–48`; module `:1–10` | Choose one canonical persistence contract, migrate/backfill safely, enforce unique indexes and add integration tests against the actual collections. |
| F-259 | P1 | Referral apply checks `referred_by`/existing invite and then separately inserts invite and updates user attribution; concurrent apply requests can create duplicate attribution or a partially applied referral without transaction/idempotency. | `src/modules/referral/referral.service.ts:90–129` | Use atomic conditional claim/unique index or transaction/outbox, explicit replay semantics and race tests. |
| F-260 | P1 | Human-friendly referral code generation uses `Math.random` and performs collision checking before a conditional write; fallback UUID/code path can return a code that was not written by that call, and code security/expiry/rate limits are not visible. | `src/modules/referral/referral.service.ts:35–55` | Use cryptographically secure bounded codes, atomic unique assignment, expiry/abuse/rate limits and verify returned code persistence. |
| F-261 | P1 | Referral dashboard returns referred users' display names and invite statuses/reward amounts without visible masking, consent, pagination cursor or retention policy; referral relationships can expose more identity than necessary. | `src/modules/referral/referral.service.ts:58–87` | Minimize/mask identity, paginate, define consent/retention policy and test cross-user dashboard isolation. |
| F-262 | P0 | Booking conversion atomically claims invite status but emits referrer/welcome reward events outside durable outbox/transaction; a crash after claim can permanently suppress one or both rewards, while event replay semantics are not recorded. | `src/modules/referral/referral.service.ts:132–148` | Couple claim and reward ledger/outbox atomically, make consumers idempotent and reconcile missing rewards with failure/replay tests. |
| F-263 | P1 | Reward amounts/types are described in comments/events but not enforced by the referral persistence model or service; the declared ReferralReward amount is unbounded and no currency/points ledger reference is stored. | `src/modules/referral/referral.service.ts:12–17,139–148`; `src/schemas/referral.schema.ts:24–44` | Use server-owned reward policy, bounded typed amounts and ledger references with approval/reversal/audit tests. |
| F-264 | P1 | Referral apply is a financial/loyalty mutation without visible Idempotency-Key, per-user rate limit, code-attempt audit or abuse controls beyond age/booking checks. | `src/modules/referral/referral.controller.ts:10–25`; service `:90–129` | Add idempotency/replay, abuse detection/rate limits and immutable attribution/audit records. |

## Loyalty findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-265 | P0 | Points award checks duplicate transaction then separately creates/updates account and transaction; concurrent/replayed events can double-award or leave balance/ledger inconsistent. | `src/modules/loyalty/loyalty.service.ts:76–124`; schema `:4–28` | Use unique `(user,reason,ref)` ledger plus atomic transaction/balance projection or idempotent event consumer and replay/race tests. |
| F-266 | P0 | Reward claim checks points, decrements account, inserts transaction, decrements stock and creates claim in separate operations; concurrent claims can overspend points, overdraw stock or leave partial claims. | `src/modules/loyalty/loyalty.service.ts:297–332`; schema `:58–87` | Atomically reserve points/stock and create claim/ledger, enforce nonnegative invariants and replay/failure tests. |
| F-267 | P1 | Expired-point sweep marks transactions swept before separately decrementing balance/creating expiry ledger; concurrent reads/sweeps can produce incorrect balances or incomplete expiry audit. | `src/modules/loyalty/loyalty.service.ts:132–157` | Make expiry reconciliation atomic/idempotent with durable sweep status and balance/ledger invariants. |
| F-268 | P1 | Challenge progress uses read/create/update and awards completion points after a separate increment; duplicate/concurrent qualifying events can overshoot progress or award challenge reward multiple times. | `src/modules/loyalty/loyalty.service.ts:202–225`; schema `:47–56` | Enforce unique user/challenge index, atomic bounded increment/completion claim and one-time reward ledger. |
| F-269 | P1 | Loyalty account/transactions/challenges/reward claims use a literal `guest` fallback under JWT, allowing shared pseudo-identity and hiding missing-auth regressions; leaderboard exposes raw user IDs. | `src/modules/loyalty/loyalty.controller.ts:21–67`; service `:262–288` | Require authenticated identity, remove guest fallback, minimize leaderboard identifiers and test unauthenticated/cross-user isolation. |
| F-270 | P1 | Displayed tier perks include cashback, free consultations, priority and personal manager benefits, but this member only calculates/display tiers and points; no benefit redemption/enforcement contract is visible. | `src/modules/loyalty/loyalty.service.ts:16–24,62–68`; controller `:13–18` | Mark unavailable benefits honestly or implement server-owned benefit contracts with eligibility/expiry/audit tests. |
| F-271 | P1 | Points, reward values, stock, counts and status/action fields are weakly bounded/free-form; reward coupon codes use `Math.random` and reward type is not enum-backed in the schema despite documented variants. | `src/modules/loyalty/loyalty.service.ts:297–332`; `src/schemas/loyalty.schemas.ts:18–43,58–87` | Add strict enums/ranges/secure token generation, expiry/reversal/refund policy and validation tests. |
| F-272 | P1 | Join/apply/claim and event consumers lack visible Idempotency-Key/request ledger, comprehensive audit actor/reason, reward reversal and retention policy; notification/event failures are swallowed. | `src/modules/loyalty/loyalty.controller.ts:45–60`; service `:113–124,246–257,297–332` | Add idempotency/outbox/audit/reversal/retention semantics and failure/replay reconciliation tests. |

## Export findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-273 | P0 | Export methods run unfiltered `find().lean()` over Users, Appointments, Orders, Transactions and AuditLogs and return database-wide PII/financial/audit data; permission is coarse and no requester/tenant/row scope is passed to the service. | `src/modules/export/export.service.ts:9–13,40–63`; controller `:7–10,19–47` | Enforce least-privilege export scopes/filters, purpose/approval, minimized columns and patient/provider/tenant isolation with owner/stranger/admin tests. |
| F-274 | P0 | Export builds the complete dataset and CSV in memory with no pagination, streaming, timeout or row/byte cap; large collections can cause memory exhaustion and operational denial of service. | `src/modules/export/export.service.ts:13–37`; controller `:19–47` | Use bounded asynchronous export jobs/streaming, caps, cancellation, backpressure and large-dataset tests. |
| F-275 | P1 | CSV escaping does not neutralize spreadsheet formula prefixes (`=`, `+`, `-`, `@`); exported patient/audit/user-controlled fields can become executable formulas when opened in spreadsheet software. | `src/modules/export/export.service.ts:18–34` | Prefix/escape dangerous cells, use safe content disposition and formula-injection regression tests. |
| F-276 | P1 | Export includes full names, phone, email, patient/doctor IDs, financial amounts, IP and user-agent without field-level minimization, visible consent/purpose logging, encryption-at-rest/download controls or retention policy. | `src/modules/export/export.service.ts:40–63`; controller `:13–16` | Define per-export schema, encrypt/protect delivery, audit access/download, expire artifacts and test PII minimization. |
| F-277 | P1 | Generic `exportToCsv(modelName, fields)` accepts arbitrary model/field names and can become a broad data-extraction primitive if reused by another caller; no allowlist is enforced inside the service. | `src/modules/export/export.service.ts:9–13`; module `:5–10` | Make model/field allowlists server-owned and test rejected model/field combinations. |
| F-278 | P1 | Filename/content-disposition is built from route constants without quoting/sanitization abstraction, and export is exposed as GET with no visible export job/audit record or idempotent request tracking. | `src/modules/export/export.controller.ts:13–16,19–47` | Harden headers, record actor/purpose/filters/rows, use explicit export lifecycle and operational audit/replay controls. |

## OCR findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-279 | P0 | OCR accepts an arbitrary remote `imageUrl` and passes it directly to Google Vision without scheme/host allowlist, signed-asset ownership, MIME/size validation or SSRF controls; the service has no actor context. | `src/modules/ocr/ocr.service.ts:18–27` | Accept only server-issued asset references, validate ownership/purpose/type/size, prevent private-network fetches and test SSRF/cross-user access. |
| F-280 | P1 | Full image URL is logged before processing and provider error objects are logged; URLs may contain signed tokens/PII and errors may contain provider/request metadata or OCR-sensitive context. | `src/modules/ocr/ocr.service.ts:23–32` | Redact URLs/secrets/errors, use structured correlation logs and test sensitive logging paths. |
| F-281 | P0 | OCR returns raw full text from medical images without field-level minimization, PHI redaction, confidence/ambiguity contract or clinical-review state; callers can treat unverified text as clinical truth. | `src/modules/ocr/ocr.service.ts:26–29` | Return typed low-trust OCR result with confidence/locale, quarantine raw text, require human/clinical review before prescribing/ordering use. |
| F-282 | P1 | Image/document processing has no visible payload/time/size limits, cancellation, rate limit, timeout or cost/quota accounting; repeated calls can create provider cost and availability abuse. | `src/modules/ocr/ocr.service.ts:18–33` | Add bounded input, timeout/circuit breaker, per-user quota/rate limit and provider cost monitoring. |
| F-283 | P1 | Missing Vision credentials are only warned during construction and the service fails later at request time; startup/health readiness does not expose an explicit fail-closed capability state. | `src/modules/ocr/ocr.service.ts:9–21` | Validate capability configuration at readiness boundary and expose truthful unavailable state without leaking secrets. |
| F-284 | P1 | No persistence, consent, retention/deletion, access audit or provenance is represented for OCR input/output; raw medical text lifecycle is left to callers with no enforced contract. | `src/modules/ocr/ocr.service.ts:18–33` | Define consented PHI lifecycle, immutable provenance/access audit, retention/legal hold and deletion semantics. |

## Bans findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-285 | P0 | Bans middleware trusts the first client-supplied `x-forwarded-for` value and any `x-device-id` header; an attacker can spoof identity to evade a ban or cause a false block of another IP/device. | `src/modules/bans/bans.middleware.ts:9–20` | Use trusted proxy configuration/normalized client IP and authenticated/attested device identity; add spoof/evasion tests. |
| F-286 | P1 | Enforcement relies on an in-memory cache loaded at module init/refreshed after local writes; multi-instance propagation, restart windows and refresh failures have no durable consistency/fail-safe contract. | `src/modules/bans/bans.service.ts:12–25,27–47`; middleware `:12–23` | Use shared cache/versioned invalidation or authoritative lookup with bounded stale policy, monitoring and multi-instance tests. |
| F-287 | P1 | `unban(value)` deactivates every ban with that value regardless of type, so an IP unban can unintentionally remove a device ban (or vice versa); route value is not encoded/typed. | `src/modules/bans/bans.controller.ts:25–28`; service `:40–43` | Require typed identity `(type,value)`, normalize values and test cross-type isolation. |
| F-288 | P1 | Ban creation has no visible normalization or uniqueness constraint for active `(type,value)`, enabling duplicates and inconsistent expiry/cache records. | `src/modules/bans/bans.service.ts:27–38`; `src/modules/bans/bans.schema.ts:7–29` | Add canonical normalization, unique active policy, conflict/idempotency semantics and duplicate tests. |
| F-289 | P1 | Ban values/reasons/admin list are returned/stored raw with no visible masking, reason bounds, access audit, retention or revocation metadata; list is unbounded. | `src/modules/bans/bans.controller.ts:30–34`; schema `:15–29`; service `:50–52` | Minimize/mask sensitive identifiers, paginate, audit administrative access, bound reason and define retention/legal hold. |
| F-290 | P1 | Global middleware throws from request path with no visible standardized error handling, cache-health behavior or observability; a cache/model failure can either deny all traffic or silently fail to enforce bans depending on caller behavior. | `src/modules/bans/bans.middleware.ts:9–25`; service `:12–25` | Define fail-safe availability/security policy, structured metrics/alerts and failure-injection tests. |
| F-291 | P1 | Ban lifecycle has `is_active`/expiry only; no immutable actor/reason/revocation event or evidence of enforcement propagation is retained, weakening incident review and compliance accountability. | `src/modules/bans/bans.schema.ts:18–29`; service `:27–44` | Add immutable audit/revocation history, effective timestamps and propagation evidence. |

## Realtime findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-292 | P0 | WebSocket authentication accepts JWT in `handshake.query.token`; query strings can leak through URLs, proxies, logs and telemetry, exposing sessions. | `src/modules/realtime/realtime.gateway.ts:41–46` | Accept token only through protected handshake auth/cookie channel, redact logs and test query-token rejection. |
| F-293 | P1 | Offline replay reads all Redis events, emits them, then deletes the list; reconnect races/crashes can duplicate or permanently lose notifications, with no per-event acknowledgment/TTL/dedup. | `src/modules/realtime/realtime.gateway.ts:74–91`; service `:20–31` | Use atomic claim/ack, bounded queue/TTL, event IDs and durable delivery/reconciliation tests. |
| F-294 | P0 | Raw internal payloads are emitted to user/role/thread rooms and raw caller/ICE/SDP data is forwarded without typed size/content validation or field-level redaction; PHI/payment metadata can overbroadcast. | `src/modules/realtime/realtime.gateway.ts:210–301,423–498`; service `:20–48` | Define event schemas/projections per audience, validate/cap signaling payloads and test role/user/thread isolation. |
| F-295 | P1 | Presence, connected socket maps and doctor waiting queues are process-local; multi-instance deployments can disagree on online state, queue order and cleanup, and restart loses operational state. | `src/modules/realtime/realtime.gateway.ts:21–26,53–60,93–124,318–421`; service `:50–70` | Use shared/versioned presence and queue state or explicitly bounded eventual consistency with failover tests. |
| F-296 | P1 | Waiting-room ETA is synthetic (`position * 15`) and broadcasts `position/eta/totalInQueue` as if operational truth without observed schedule/queue timing; users may receive misleading wait estimates. | `src/modules/realtime/realtime.gateway.ts:389–409` | Derive from authoritative scheduling/observed service state or label estimate honestly and test state changes. |
| F-297 | P1 | Presence and waiting-room handlers accept arrays/IDs/payloads with no visible length/rate/format limits; heartbeat, bulk presence and signaling can be abused for resource or event amplification. | `src/modules/realtime/realtime.gateway.ts:128–139,212–301,320–377` | Add schema validation, per-socket rate/size limits, backpressure and abuse tests. |
| F-298 | P1 | Call authorization is delegated to LiveKit but caller-controlled `caller_name`, `call_type`, ICE and SDP are forwarded; there is no visible session expiry/audience binding or output sanitization in gateway. | `src/modules/realtime/realtime.gateway.ts:212–301` | Bind signaling to server-issued session/participants/expiry, ignore client identity labels and validate bounded protocol payloads. |
| F-299 | P1 | Internal event fanout emits admin role events and user notifications/payments from raw payloads with no visible durable delivery/audit contract; downstream failures are not surfaced as a truthful delivery state. | `src/modules/realtime/realtime.gateway.ts:423–498`; service `:30,45–48` | Add audience-specific DTOs, delivery IDs/ack/retry/audit and reconciliation/PII tests. |

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-300 | P0 | Report creation accepts raw `body.patient_id` and persists the report for that patient; a permitted provider can create a report against an arbitrary patient without a visible appointment/relationship/consent check. | `src/modules/medical-reports/medical-reports.service.ts:32–57`; controller `:46–50` | Resolve patient/encounter from authorized clinical relationship, validate consent and immutable provenance, and test cross-patient creation. |
