| F-302 | P1 | Detail lookup returns full report object (including body and attachments) to patient/admin and mutates viewed state during GET; there is no role-specific projection or immutable read audit. | `src/modules/medical-reports/medical-reports.service.ts:24–30`; schema `:47–52` | Define minimized projections by role, separate read receipt mutation/idempotency and audit PHI access. |
| F-303 | P1 | `track/:trackingId` is described as public-ish/share/print but remains JWT-protected and exposes a tracking identifier lookup; contract is ambiguous and lacks share token scope/expiry/revocation semantics. | `src/modules/medical-reports/medical-reports.controller.ts:41–43`; service `:62–68` | Define authenticated vs share contract, use scoped expiring share tokens and test stranger/revocation/expiry. |
| F-304 | P1 | Create emits `medical_report.created` after persistence without transaction/outbox/idempotency; retries/crashes can duplicate reports or leave timeline/notification consumers inconsistent. | `src/modules/medical-reports/medical-reports.service.ts:32–60`; controller `:49–51` | Add idempotency key/unique encounter version and durable outbox/reconciliation tests. |
| F-305 | P1 | The controller's duplicate timeline independently queries five collections and converts all query failures to empty arrays; a partial outage is presented as an honest empty medical history and differs from the unified Timeline surface. | `src/modules/medical-reports/medical-reports.controller.ts:14–33` | Use one canonical timeline contract, surface partial/degraded status and test collection failure/consistency behavior. |
| F-306 | P1 | Timeline labels radiology bookings as `type: 'lab'` with a separate kind field, creating contract ambiguity for clients and analytics. | `src/modules/medical-reports/medical-reports.controller.ts:27–30` | Use canonical discriminated event types and compatibility/version tests. |
| F-307 | P1 | MedicalReport schema contains PHI, linked identities, attachment data and patient-view fields but has no visible consent, provenance/version/correction, retention/legal-hold or deletion policy; `critical` is a free clinical flag without workflow. | `src/schemas/medical-report.schema.ts:22–56` | Implement immutable clinical versioning/correction, critical-result workflow, access/retention controls and deletion/legal-hold tests. |

## Chat findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-308 | P0 | Chat permissions endpoint loads a thread and can return active chat/call/upload permissions before asserting the requester is a participant; a valid JWT can probe or receive policy for another thread. | `src/modules/chat/chat.module.ts:24–41`; service `:100–103` | Assert participant/relationship before any permission response and test owner/stranger/unauth 404/401 semantics. |
| F-309 | P0 | WebSocket `send_message`, `initiate_call` and `mark_seen` handlers emit directly without ChatService participant/status/media checks; caller-controlled `state` can bypass clinical lifecycle restrictions and messages are not persisted through the authoritative service. | `src/modules/chat/chat.gateway.ts:89–154`; service `:204–287` | Route all handlers through server-derived session/thread authorization, ignore client state, persist/audit mutations and test cross-room BOLA. |
| F-310 | P1 | Direct/group thread creation and participant management allow an existing participant to add/remove arbitrary user IDs without visible relationship, invitation, consent or admin/owner policy. | `src/modules/chat/chat.service.ts:25–40,402–420`; module `:142–155,219–226` | Define thread owner/role/invitation policy, validate participant existence/relationship and test unauthorized membership changes. |
| F-311 | P0 | Chat message/thread metadata updates, message creation, unread counters and event emissions are separate operations; crash/retry can create inconsistent messages/counters/realtime notifications, and REST idempotency is only visible on send route. | `src/modules/chat/chat.service.ts:229–285`; module `:173–192` | Use durable idempotency/event outbox or transaction/reconciliation, cover replay/failure/concurrency paths. |
| F-312 | P1 | Chat and message schemas store body, attachment metadata, participant IDs, reactions, read/delivery arrays and linked media without visible encryption, retention/legal-hold, immutable audit or field-level role projection. | `src/modules/chat/chat.schemas.ts:7–58`; service `:289–399` | Define PHI-safe projections/encryption/retention/access audit and test provider/patient/admin visibility. |
| F-313 | P1 | `ChatGatewayGuard` trusts body `senderId` for the waiting-to-live transition and grants FAMILY sessions a complete constraint bypass; guard is HTTP-body based and is not visibly wired to every WebSocket handler. | `src/modules/chat/guards/chat-gateway.guard.ts:15–34`; `:22–24` | Derive actor from verified auth context, scope family policy explicitly, wire guard consistently and test impersonation/bypass. |
| F-314 | P1 | Chat service catches booking model lookup failures and returns permissive communication, so an unavailable appointment registry can fail open and allow chat outside clinical eligibility. | `src/modules/chat/chat.service.ts:161–199`; `:46–64` | Fail closed or surface degraded state for clinical authorization failures and add dependency-failure tests. |
| F-315 | P1 | Chat schemas and handlers accept unbounded body/edit/search/name/participant arrays, attachment metadata and voice duration; no comprehensive rate, size, MIME, content or abuse limits are visible outside media ID count. | `src/modules/chat/chat.service.ts:204–246,289–307,333–369`; schemas `:29–58` | Add typed bounded DTOs, content/MIME/rate controls, moderation and load/abuse tests. |
| F-316 | P1 | Follow-up closure is enforced hourly from `updatedAt`, while service permission uses appointment completion time/config; concurrent updates and scheduler lag can produce divergent chat availability and stale sessions. | `src/modules/chat/schedulers/chat-lifecycle.scheduler.ts:11–27`; module `:86–121`; service `:168–194` | Use one authoritative time/state machine, atomic closure and reconciliation/clock-boundary tests. |

## AdminController findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-317 | P0 | Admin referral/loyalty reports and user 360 endpoint expose platform-wide identity, phone, financial points, SOS location, provider license/profile changes and activity under a coarse controller-wide ADMIN role without visible field-level purpose/scope/minimization. | `src/modules/admin/admin.controller.ts:39–131,139–235` | Apply least-privilege admin scopes, field minimization, step-up access and immutable PHI/financial access audit. |
| F-318 | P1 | Owner authorization falls back to a hard-coded email and sub-admin mutation returns a generated initial password in the response; credential delivery/rotation and owner identity are not bound to a managed immutable control. | `src/modules/admin/admin.controller.ts:319–324,348–375` | Use managed owner identity/step-up, one-time credential enrollment, no password response and secret-delivery audit. |
| F-319 | P1 | Admin and provider creation accept raw `any` bodies; provider identity/license/contact fields and permissions lack comprehensive DTO validation, normalization, uniqueness/rate/idempotency controls. | `src/modules/admin/admin.controller.ts:348–372,423–454` | Use typed allowlisted DTOs, canonical validation, approval workflow, idempotency and abuse tests. |
| F-320 | P0 | Permanent user deletion performs multiple collection `deleteMany` operations followed by user deletion without transaction, manifest, preflight or rollback; partial failure can leave privacy residues or inconsistent references. | `src/modules/admin/admin.controller.ts:490–523` | Use transactional/queued deletion with inventory, verification, retry/reconciliation, legal hold and failure-injection tests. |
| F-321 | P1 | Ban/unban and provider approve/suspend use separate read/save transitions with best-effort event emission and no visible idempotency, audit event durability or synchronization across User/provider account/profile state. | `src/modules/admin/admin.controller.ts:457–484,529–552` | Implement atomic state machine, durable audit/outbox, idempotency and cross-model consistency tests. |
| F-322 | P0 | Provider delta approval returns success after changing only delta status; the code explicitly leaves application of `requested_changes` to ProviderProfile unimplemented, producing a false-success moderation contract. | `src/modules/admin/admin.controller.ts:555–575` | Apply changes transactionally or return blocked status; add approval/rejection/application verification tests. |
| F-323 | P1 | Provider delta retrieval is exposed as POST and returns raw pending deltas, including old/new/requested data, with no visible projection, pagination, ownership or sensitive-field masking. | `src/modules/admin/admin.controller.ts:555–561` | Use canonical GET/read contract, bounded/paginated DTOs, field-level permissions and audit. |
| F-324 | P1 | User directory and referral/loyalty views perform free-text identity search and return names/phone/email-related data with no visible anti-enumeration, export/access audit or rate limits beyond page size. | `src/modules/admin/admin.controller.ts:251–305`; reports `:39–96` | Add scoped search, rate limits, minimization, audit and anti-enumeration tests. |
| F-325 | P1 | Admin routes use raw IDs/queries with mixed `findOne({id})`/`findById()` and broad collections/model access, while unimplemented disputes intentionally throw ServiceUnavailable; route contracts and operational readiness are inconsistent. | `src/modules/admin/admin.controller.ts:139–145,242–245,457–541` | Publish a complete admin contract with identifier validation, truthful unavailable states and route/security regression tests. |

## InsuranceController findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-326 | P1 | `GET /insurance/active` returns the stored `insurance_details` object with `additionalProperties: true`, exposing an unconstrained policy/PII projection and making response shape depend on arbitrary persisted fields. | `src/modules/insurance/insurance.controller.ts:25–50` | Define a strict allowlisted patient projection, redact sensitive fields, and test cross-user/field leakage. |
| F-327 | P1 | The active-policy query relies on `req.user.id` and guard composition but the controller has no explicit access audit, profile existence contract, or identifier/schema validation; guard regressions could change the ownership boundary silently. | `src/modules/insurance/insurance.controller.ts:17–23,48–50` | Add explicit ownership contract, access audit and 401/403/empty/profile-drift tests. |
| F-328 | P1 | Insurance company and plan responses allow `additionalProperties: true` at both levels, so internal/admin fields can leak and clients lack a stable versioned catalog contract. | `src/modules/insurance/insurance.controller.ts:58–90` | Use strict company/plan DTOs, field minimization, version/freshness metadata and schema regression tests. |
| F-329 | P1 | Companies catalog is protected by `NoGuestsGuard` despite being described as a single source for multiple app surfaces; the controller itself does not document/cache/freshness/deprecation behavior, risking client divergence and inconsistent accessibility. | `src/modules/insurance/insurance.controller.ts:16–18,53–63,93–97` | Publish an explicit audience/auth contract, cache/version semantics and parity tests for patient/provider/admin consumers. |

## PresenceService findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-330 | P1 | Online/offline updates are a read-modify-write sequence across Redis set/hash keys; concurrent disconnects or multi-device transitions can mark a user offline while another socket remains active or leave stale device membership. | `src/modules/presence/presence.service.ts:22–49` | Use atomic multi-key transition/script or versioned reconciliation and test concurrent device connect/disconnect. |
| F-331 | P1 | `heartbeat(userId,socketId)` refreshes presence keys without verifying that the socket belongs to the user/device set, so a weak caller boundary can keep another account online indefinitely. | `src/modules/presence/presence.service.ts:51–54` | Bind heartbeat to authenticated socket context and verify membership; test cross-user/socket spoofing. |
| F-332 | P1 | User and socket IDs are used directly in Redis keys and presence responses without normalization/namespace privacy controls; device counts and last-seen data can become a cross-tenant/key-injection or information-disclosure surface if inputs are not constrained upstream. | `src/modules/presence/presence.service.ts:19–20,63–72` | Canonicalize identifiers, isolate namespaces, minimize output and enforce upstream schema/ownership. |
| F-333 | P1 | `getBulkPresence` performs unbounded `Promise.all` over caller-supplied IDs, enabling amplification and latency/resource exhaustion; no maximum list size, deduplication or rate limit is visible. | `src/modules/presence/presence.service.ts:75–77` | Bound/deduplicate requests, batch Redis operations, rate limit and add load/abuse tests. |
| F-334 | P1 | Redis failures and TTL expiry have no explicit truthful/degraded contract; callers can receive offline/zero last-seen values that may represent infrastructure failure rather than actual user state. | `src/modules/presence/presence.service.ts:56–81` | Distinguish unavailable from offline, instrument health/freshness and test Redis outage/expiry behavior. |

## Unified Bookings findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-335 | P0 | The co-located controller exposes root consultation reschedule as `POST :id/reschedule`, while the published live contract in the audit context requires `PATCH /unified-bookings/consultation/{id}/reschedule`; method/path drift can make a valid client call fail or hit another route. | `src/modules/unified-bookings/unified-bookings.module.ts:408–422` | Verify every method/path against live backend, publish one canonical route and contract tests. |
| F-336 | P0 | Cart checkout forwards client-provided item names/prices and payment/insurance metadata into lab/radiology/pharmacy domain calls; the unified layer does not visibly reprice from server catalog or bind insurance/payment truth to a server quote. | `src/modules/unified-bookings/unified-bookings.module.ts:304–375` | Re-resolve catalog/price/coverage server-side, reject stale/mismatched quotes and test tampered totals/payment methods. |
| F-337 | P0 | Multi-domain checkout creates groups independently and compensates by cancelling prior successes; rollback failures are ignored and cart clearing is separate, so a partial financial/service state can remain while the response reports `rolled_back`. | `src/modules/unified-bookings/unified-bookings.module.ts:315–395` | Use durable saga/outbox/reconciliation with explicit per-group truth, no false rollback claim and failure-injection tests. |
| F-338 | P1 | Lab/radiology/nursing reschedule directly changes `scheduled_at` after only checking ownership/state, without visible provider-slot availability, lock, conflict detection or idempotent transition; concurrent requests can double-book. | `src/modules/unified-bookings/unified-bookings.module.ts:127–160` | Resolve and atomically reserve an available slot with idempotency/version checks and replay/concurrency tests. |
| F-339 | P1 | The standalone Redis lock helper uses a 300-second lock and unconditional delete release without owner-token verification; a late releaser can delete another request's lock, and the helper is separate from the wired orchestrator. | `src/modules/unified-bookings/unified-bookings.service.ts:13–30` | Use owner-token compare-and-delete, one canonical lock implementation and expiry/concurrency tests. |
| F-340 | P1 | Nursing radius broadcast performs a separate booking and provider assignment transition, catches transition errors and still returns a successful-looking booking/provider result; the response can misstate assignment. | `src/modules/unified-bookings/unified-bookings.module.ts:258–297` | Return authoritative assignment state/error, make transition atomic/idempotent and test failure paths. |
| F-341 | P1 | Consultation contract intentionally rejects all non-cash payment methods, while checkout and client contracts may offer other methods; unsupported payment is a truthful block but leaves the booking journey incomplete for card/insurance flows. | `src/modules/unified-bookings/unified-bookings.module.ts:182–205` | Publish capability matrix and implement/keep blocked non-cash flows without UI success/fallback; add contract tests. |
| F-342 | P1 | Unified timeline derives totals, titles, payment method and cancellability with fallbacks such as `0` and `'cash'`, and merges duplicate domain collections including two pharmacy sources; clients can see incorrect financial/service semantics. | `src/modules/unified-bookings/unified-bookings.module.ts:60–100` | Use canonical source projections, explicit unknown/null values and deduplication/consistency tests. |
| F-343 | P1 | Generic `kind/id` reads and mutations rely on a map and raw IDs/body fields with no visible strict identifier/DTO validation; unknown or alias kinds can drift across domain contracts and error semantics. | `src/modules/unified-bookings/unified-bookings.module.ts:103–125,416–426` | Use typed discriminated DTOs, canonical identifiers and owner/stranger/unauth/method tests per kind. |
| F-344 | P1 | Root cancel/reschedule/checkout operations mix idempotency decorators with domain calls and best-effort event emission, but no durable idempotency/result binding or cross-domain transaction boundary is visible. | `src/modules/unified-bookings/unified-bookings.module.ts:116–160,304–395,399–429` | Persist request/result fingerprints and authoritative transition events; test replay, timeout, duplicate and reconciliation paths. |

## LiveKit findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-345 | P0 | `POST /calls/webhook` returns `{received:true}` while verification is explicitly unimplemented; forged LiveKit events can be accepted as trusted integration signals. | `src/modules/livekit/livekit.controller.ts:31–35` | Verify signed raw webhook payload, reject invalid/replay events, persist idempotently and test forged/duplicate events. |
| F-346 | P1 | Legacy `createToken` issues a two-hour room token from caller-supplied room and participant name alongside the narrower booking token, creating an inconsistent token policy and room/identity confusion. | `src/modules/livekit/livekit.service.ts:21–45` | Remove/segregate legacy capability, derive room/identity server-side and enforce one documented TTL/audience policy. |
| F-347 | P0 | Provider waiting-room, ping-patient and no-show routes have JWT but no visible provider-role/appointment relationship decorator at controller boundary; service checks are inconsistent and `markNoShow` can mutate by mixed ID/filter semantics. | `src/modules/livekit/livekit.controller.ts:13–28`; service `:69–137` | Enforce provider role and appointment ownership/assignment centrally, add idempotent state transition and owner/stranger tests. |
| F-348 | P1 | Call initiation inserts `callsessions` after issuing a token and uses caller-supplied caller name/call type; failure between token/event/insert leaves an unusable or untracked room and identity labels can be spoofed. | `src/modules/livekit/livekit.service.ts:159–185` | Create authoritative session first, derive identity/type from appointment/auth, issue token after commit and reconcile failures. |
| F-349 | P1 | Join/end/reject transitions use separate conditional updates with no visible idempotency/result binding or terminal-state/event reconciliation; duplicate requests and races can produce contradictory call status/duration. | `src/modules/livekit/livekit.service.ts:188–217` | Use atomic state machine with transition preconditions, idempotency and concurrent lifecycle tests. |
| F-350 | P1 | `saveMetrics` accepts arbitrary metrics and writes them without schema, bounds, size/rate limits or field projection; call diagnostics can be abused for storage/PII pollution and are not visibly audited. | `src/modules/livekit/livekit.service.ts:219–225`; controller `:61–68` | Define bounded metrics DTO/version, rate/size controls, retention and access audit. |
| F-351 | P0 | `getActiveRooms` and `getCallAnalytics` are service-wide reads; controller role decorators exist for these admin routes, but no visible field minimization/audit limits exposure of room names, appointment IDs and participant IDs. | `src/modules/livekit/livekit.service.ts:259–298`; controller `:80–112` | Apply least-privilege admin scope, minimize identifiers, paginate/audit and test admin/non-admin isolation. |
| F-352 | P1 | LiveKit room participant operations catch remote errors and return `{success:true}` for removal, while unconfigured/list failures collapse to empty/unavailable values; caller cannot distinguish completed action from provider failure. | `src/modules/livekit/livekit.service.ts:300–341` | Return truthful provider outcome, persist command status/retry and test remote/API failure. |
| F-353 | P1 | Session identity/room access has mixed appointment/provider field aliases and `createToken` accepts arbitrary room names; without a unique room/participant invariant, tokens can be issued for unintended rooms or identities. | `src/modules/livekit/livekit.service.ts:21–31,52–67,159–185`; module `:7–17` | Canonicalize identifiers/room naming, bind token grant to persisted session/appointment and test alias/room collision cases. |

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-354 | P0 | Legacy `AppSocketGateway` has no visible handshake authentication or guard and globally broadcasts arbitrary `sendMessage` payloads to every connected socket. | `src/modules/socket/socket.gateway.ts:6–23` | Retire the gateway or enforce authenticated, typed, audience-scoped events; test unauthenticated connection and global leakage. |
| F-355 | P0 | Any client can join `provider_<id>` or `patient_<id>` rooms using a supplied identifier without ownership/relationship checks, enabling room eavesdropping and event interception. | `src/modules/socket/socket.gateway.ts:27–37` | Remove legacy joins or derive/authorize room membership server-side; add owner/stranger tests. |

| F-357 | P1 | Room names and identifiers are built from raw client strings with no validation/normalization/encoding, allowing malformed identifiers and possible room namespace collisions. | `src/modules/socket/socket.gateway.ts:27–37` | Validate canonical IDs, encode room keys and test collision/injection cases. |
| F-358 | P1 | Gateway has no visible rate, payload-size, connection-limit, replay, acknowledgement or backpressure controls; unauthenticated/global handlers are an amplification surface. | `src/modules/socket/socket.gateway.ts:11–23,27–45` | Add lifecycle abuse controls or retire the surface; test flood/oversize/reconnect behavior. |
| F-359 | P0 | A parallel legacy gateway duplicates Realtime/Chat surfaces with incompatible event names and weaker controls, so clients may connect to an ungoverned path despite secure canonical routes. | `src/modules/socket/socket.gateway.ts:6–45`; compare realtime/chat gateway surfaces | Declare one canonical transport, disable legacy registration, inventory clients and add duplicate-surface regression checks. |

## System Health Controller findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-360 | P0 | Liveness endpoint is unauthenticated and returns hard-coded `database: connected`, `redis: connected`, `core_api: running` without probing any dependency; it can falsely mark a failed system healthy and exposes operational assertions publicly. | `src/modules/admin-web-core/controllers/system-health.controller.ts:3–17` | Separate public liveness from protected/internal readiness, perform truthful bounded probes and test dependency outage responses. |
| F-361 | P0 | Readiness endpoint always returns `status: ok` and never checks configuration, database, Redis, queues, external providers or migrations, so deployment/load-balancer gates can route traffic to an unready instance. | `src/modules/admin-web-core/controllers/system-health.controller.ts:19–26` | Implement dependency/readiness contract with appropriate HTTP status, timeout and degraded states; add startup/ outage tests. |
| F-362 | P1 | Health responses expose uptime and precise timestamps without visible access/audit policy, and the controller has no role/network restriction despite residing under an admin-web-core surface. | `src/modules/admin-web-core/controllers/system-health.controller.ts:3–26` | Minimize operational telemetry, restrict management probes and audit access where needed. |
| F-363 | P1 | Liveness/readiness semantics are collapsed into static success responses, providing no distinction between process health and dependency readiness or capability state. | `src/modules/admin-web-core/controllers/system-health.controller.ts:6–25` | Publish canonical probe semantics/versioned schema and monitor false-positive/false-negative health outcomes. |

| F-372 | P1 | Public/admin flag responses return repository documents directly without strict projection or bounded schema, so future internal fields can leak and clients can become coupled to persistence shape. | `src/modules/feature-flags/feature-flags.service.ts:19–20`; controller `:15–16,27–29` | Use strict response DTOs, field minimization and schema regression tests. |

| F-374 | P1 | Remote flags are described as changing app behavior without an app update, but no visible targeting, staged rollout, kill-switch audit, dependency compatibility or client-safe default contract is present. | `src/modules/feature-flags/feature-flags.controller.ts:12–16`; service `:10–20` | Define rollout/compatibility metadata, safe defaults, emergency change audit and mobile/web parity tests. |

## System Config Governance findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-380 | P0 | Global system configuration is returned as an unrestricted `value` object; any sensitive operational or secret-like fields stored there can be disclosed to an admin without field-level projection/redaction in the controller contract. | `src/modules/admin-governance/system-config.controller.ts:19–29` | Define typed, field-scoped config DTOs, redact secrets and enforce per-setting permissions/audit. |
| F-381 | P0 | `PUT` accepts raw `value:any` and replaces the complete singleton configuration via upsert, allowing arbitrary nested settings and accidental deletion of unrelated controls. | `src/modules/admin-governance/system-config.controller.ts:31–36` | Use allowlisted patch DTOs, validation, immutable/versioned updates and protected setting workflows. |
| F-382 | P1 | Configuration update has no visible idempotency, optimistic concurrency, maker-checker, actor/reason audit or durable change event; concurrent admins can silently lose changes. | `src/modules/admin-governance/system-config.controller.ts:31–36` | Add version preconditions, idempotent request/result binding, durable audit/outbox and replay/conflict tests. |
| F-383 | P1 | `GET` mutates state by auto-creating the singleton document when absent, conflating read and initialization and potentially masking migration/deployment/configuration failures. | `src/modules/admin-governance/system-config.controller.ts:19–27` | Move initialization to controlled migration/startup, make GET side-effect-free and test missing-config failure semantics. |
| F-384 | P1 | The singleton key and returned value have no visible schema/version/effective-time/source contract, so consumers cannot distinguish stale, partial or incompatible configuration. | `src/modules/admin-governance/system-config.controller.ts:21–35` | Publish versioned config schema with provenance/effective time and compatibility/rollback tests. |

| F-417 | P1 | Mental-health mutations have no visible idempotency/replay protection, audit event or rate limit, allowing duplicate mood/practice/contact writes and abuse of sensitive endpoints. | `src/modules/mental-health/mental-health.controller.ts:20–25,40–44,58–62,78–82; src/modules/mental-health/mental-health.service.ts:105–111,145–164,180–197,213–231` | Add idempotency where client retries can duplicate records, abuse controls and durable actor/audit evidence. |
| F-418 | P1 | Mood history can return up to 365 days including notes and tags with no visible pagination, projection, retention policy or export/deletion governance. | `src/modules/mental-health/mental-health.controller.ts:27–32; src/modules/mental-health/mental-health.service.ts:114–122` | Bound and project sensitive history, document retention/deletion, and test large histories and PII minimization. |
| F-419 | P1 | Create endpoints and crisis-contact reads return full persisted documents, potentially exposing internal identifiers or sensitive fields beyond the minimum UI contract. | `src/modules/mental-health/mental-health.service.ts:105–111,157–164,190–197,207–210,224–231` | Use strict response DTOs and field-level redaction tests. |
| F-420 | P1 | `getMeditationStats` loads all patient sessions and the dashboard performs multiple independent queries, creating unbounded resource use and a potentially inconsistent snapshot. | `src/modules/mental-health/mental-health.service.ts:172–177,242–250` | Aggregate with bounded database queries, indexes and consistent snapshot semantics; test volume/failure behavior. |
| F-421 | P1 | The mental-health module has no visible retention, immutable audit, patient export or patient-directed deletion workflow for highly sensitive wellbeing records and crisis contacts. | `src/modules/mental-health/mental-health.module.ts:16–34; src/modules/mental-health/mental-health.service.ts:105–251` | Define lifecycle/DSAR controls, audit access and test deletion/export authorization and recovery. |

## Maternity findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-422 | P0 | Maternity mutations update embedded arrays using read/modify/save with no visible idempotency, version predicate, atomic operator or audit, so retries/concurrent devices can duplicate or lose kick, contraction, checkup or infant-growth events. | `src/modules/maternity/maternity.service.ts:92–154` | Use atomic/versioned operations, idempotency and audit; test replay and concurrent updates. |
| F-423 | P1 | Maternity profile and log operations return the full persisted profile document, potentially exposing reproductive history, dates, checkups and infant measurements beyond the minimum response contract. | `src/modules/maternity/maternity.service.ts:51–53,92–116,118–126,129–154` | Define strict patient response DTOs and field-level minimization tests. |
| F-424 | P1 | `GET /maternity/profile` performs a persistence write to refresh `current_week`, violating read-only semantics and creating hidden write/race behavior. | `src/modules/maternity/maternity.service.ts:46–50` | Move derived-week computation to read projection or explicit job; test GET idempotence and failure handling. |
| F-425 | P1 | `GET /maternity/content` returns empty content arrays while presenting a successful response, creating a feature gap and risking a misleading “available” experience. | `src/modules/maternity/maternity.controller.ts:24–27; src/modules/maternity/maternity.service.ts:38–41` | Expose explicit unavailable/curation status or publish reviewed, localized content; test truthfulness. |
| F-426 | P1 | Pregnancy date validation accepts any parseable date before applying a narrow estimate window; cycle dates and cross-field relationships are not fully constrained, allowing semantically inconsistent reproductive profiles. | `src/modules/maternity/maternity.service.ts:21–24,55–90` | Validate timezone/date boundaries, LMP/due-date consistency and profile state transitions with contract tests. |
| F-427 | P1 | Infant-growth updates use truthiness checks, so a valid numeric value that is falsy after coercion is not applied consistently; embedded records also use undefined IDs and have no duplicate/concurrency contract. | `src/modules/maternity/maternity.service.ts:129–149` | Use explicit `!== undefined` updates, stable identifiers and atomic unique-month semantics; test partial updates/replays. |
| F-428 | P1 | The maternity surface has no visible clinical-safety escalation, red-flag guidance, reminder/notification integration, retention/deletion or immutable audit despite handling pregnancy, contractions and infant measurements. | `src/modules/maternity/maternity.controller.ts:18–58; src/modules/maternity/maternity.service.ts:92–154` | Define non-diagnostic safety UX, escalation boundaries, lifecycle controls and audited access. |

## Nutrition findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-429 | P1 | Nutrition profile, meal, water and exercise mutations have no visible idempotency/replay protection, audit event, rate limit or deduplication, allowing duplicate health logs on client retries. | `src/modules/nutrition/nutrition.controller.ts:27–30,33–37,53–58,66–70; src/modules/nutrition/nutrition.service.ts:75–109,114–131,146–152,167–179` | Add idempotency/abuse controls and durable audit where required; test replay and concurrent devices. |
| F-430 | P1 | Nutrition reads and writes return full persisted profile/log documents, exposing allergies, dietary restrictions, measurements and user-entered nutrition data beyond the minimum contract. | `src/modules/nutrition/nutrition.service.ts:69–73,103–110,114–131,146–153,167–179` | Use strict response DTOs, field-level minimization and privacy tests. |
| F-431 | P1 | Meal, water and exercise history queries are unbounded by pagination and projection, while date parsing uses local timezone construction without an explicit canonical timezone contract. | `src/modules/nutrition/nutrition.service.ts:29–35,134–142,155–163,181–188` | Bound/projection queries, define timezone semantics and test DST/locale boundaries and large histories. |
| F-432 | P1 | Weekly report performs seven sequential daily-summary calls, each issuing four reads, creating an N+1-style latency/load pattern and possible cross-query inconsistency. | `src/modules/nutrition/nutrition.service.ts:242–281` | Replace with bounded aggregation/snapshot semantics and test worst-case latency/failure behavior. |
| F-433 | P1 | Calories, macros and calories-burned are accepted from the client with no visible provenance, canonical food/exercise source or plausibility relationship checks, so summaries may be numerically consistent but not truthful. | `src/modules/nutrition/nutrition.service.ts:114–130,167–177,204–219` | Define authoritative data provenance, cross-field plausibility checks and explicit estimate labels. |
| F-434 | P1 | Profile readiness is derived from goal, daily calorie target and water target only, without validating that targets are clinically appropriate for the person or consistent with measurements/activity. | `src/modules/nutrition/nutrition.service.ts:98–109` | Mark recommendations as estimates, define clinical-safety boundaries and prevent unsafe target claims. |
| F-435 | P1 | The nutrition surface has no visible retention/deletion/export/audit lifecycle for allergies, restrictions, body measurements and daily health logs. | `src/modules/nutrition/nutrition.service.ts:69–281; src/modules/nutrition/nutrition.module.ts:16–28` | Define patient lifecycle/DSAR controls, access audit and recovery tests. |

## Redis findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-436 | P0 | Redis failure silently activates a per-process fallback for stateful primitives, so locks, idempotency, rate limits and security counters can diverge across replicas and may fail open during outage. | `src/modules/redis/redis.service.ts:15–23,105–159,308–313` | Classify primitives; fail closed for security/financial locks and test multi-replica outage behavior. |
| F-437 | P1 | Fallback implementations use non-atomic read/modify/write semantics for increment, setnx, sets and sorted sets, unlike Redis atomic commands, creating race-condition behavior under concurrent requests. | `src/modules/redis/redis.service.ts:139–159,218–277` | Use atomic scripts/transactions or disable fallback for correctness-critical operations; run concurrency tests. |
| F-438 | P1 | The shared `ready` flag is set true by any of three connections and false by any error, so client/subscriber/publisher health is not independently represented and event/state behavior can be misclassified. | `src/modules/redis/redis.service.ts:28–59` | Track connection roles independently and expose truthful health/readiness semantics. |
| F-439 | P1 | Pub/sub events are silently dropped when Redis is unavailable and subscribe returns without registration, with no durable replay/outbox or caller-visible degraded state. | `src/modules/redis/redis.service.ts:279–296` | Add durable event delivery/replay or explicit loss semantics and outage tests for notifications/workflows. |
| F-440 | P1 | `getClient()` exposes a partial raw client/shim whose fallback commands use storage semantics different from native hash/set/zset methods; callers can obtain `PONG` while Redis is actually unavailable. | `src/modules/redis/redis.service.ts:315–374` | Encapsulate command contracts, remove misleading health responses and test every advanced call-site against outage semantics. |
| F-441 | P1 | `checkRateLimit` performs `incr` then `expire` without atomic script/transaction and inherits fallback divergence, allowing window drift and race-based limit bypass. | `src/modules/redis/redis.service.ts:308–313` | Implement atomic fixed/sliding-window limit semantics with failover and concurrent tests. |
| F-442 | P1 | Connected `keys(pattern)` uses Redis `KEYS`, which can block Redis on large keyspaces and expose broad key-enumeration capability to callers. | `src/modules/redis/redis.service.ts:161–167` | Replace with bounded SCAN abstraction, restrict patterns and load-test keyspace behavior. |
| F-443 | P1 | `getJson` propagates malformed stored JSON without a typed corruption/degraded policy, while raw key/value APIs have no visible namespace, size, serialization or secret-value governance. | `src/modules/redis/redis.service.ts:298–305; src/modules/redis/redis.service.ts:105–167` | Add typed serialization/error handling, key namespace/TTL policies and secret/logging tests. |

## Emergency and ambulance fleet findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-444 | P0 | SOS trigger persists patient name, phone, symptoms and precise location; patient-facing projections still return symptoms/location/paramedic identity with no visible consent, retention, redaction or sensitive-scope control. | `src/modules/emergency/emergency.service.ts:39–55,129–148; src/modules/emergency/emergency.controller.ts:7–15` | Enforce emergency-specific consent/access policy, minimize/redact fields, define retention and test cross-role disclosure. |
| F-445 | P0 | Auto-dispatch is fire-and-forget after SOS creation; dispatch failure is swallowed, so a successful SOS response can leave a patient without an acknowledged dispatch/degraded status. | `src/modules/emergency/emergency.service.ts:141–148` | Persist dispatch state/outcome, surface truthful pending/unavailable status and alert operators on failure. |
| F-446 | P1 | Dispatch scoring performs per-vehicle provider and workload queries and considers persisted rating/city/GPS without visible freshness, transaction, candidate cap or audit contract, creating load and explainability risks. | `src/modules/emergency/emergency.service.ts:63–107` | Bound/aggregate candidate evaluation, define data freshness/override policy and audit dispatch decisions. |
| F-447 | P1 | Emergency state/history transitions are inconsistent: some paths read/save, some update directly without state predicates/history entries, and cancellation records an empty `from` state. | `src/modules/emergency/emergency.service.ts:150–183,194–209,244–249` | Centralize an atomic state machine with version predicates, complete history and concurrency tests. |
| F-448 | P1 | Tracking and unit GPS accept finite coordinates but do not validate latitude/longitude ranges, stale timestamps or update frequency; ETA uses a fixed urban-speed assumption that may appear authoritative. | `src/modules/emergency/emergency.service.ts:264–298,301–314` | Validate spatial/time bounds, freshness and rate limits; label ETA as estimate and test malformed/stale GPS. |
| F-449 | P1 | Driver missions expose an unassigned SOS pool to any JWT caller reaching the route because no visible role assertion exists in `EmergencyController`; service filters provider-owned missions but pool access remains broad. | `src/modules/emergency/emergency.controller.ts:29–33; src/modules/emergency/emergency.service.ts:221–235` | Enforce driver/ambulance/facility role and provider scope before pool access; test patient/non-driver denial. |
| F-450 | P1 | Ambulance fleet updates accept documents, availability, metadata and last_location with weak field validation and no visible idempotency/version/audit; duplicate plate protection is application-level and can race. | `src/modules/emergency/ambulance-fleet.controller.ts:24–58` | Add typed DTOs, document/GPS validation, unique DB constraint, version/audit and replay/concurrency tests. |
| F-451 | P1 | Fleet/admin operations return broad vehicle documents and raw admin notes; admin emergency record/mutation routes are intentionally unavailable, leaving an operational control-plane gap that must remain explicitly blocked. | `src/modules/emergency/ambulance-fleet.controller.ts:20–22,42,57,71–80,125–142; src/modules/emergency/emergency.controller.ts:53–81` | Use strict projections/redaction, bounded notes and an approved emergency governance contract before enabling admin mutations. |

## SMS findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-452 | P0 | SMS activation is controlled by a runtime feature flag read from a shared collection, but this service shows no actor authorization, change audit, approval or emergency rollback guard. | `src/modules/sms/sms.service.ts:23–32` | Gate flag changes through audited admin governance, scoped permissions and tested rollback. |
| F-453 | P1 | Provider readiness is misleading: the code treats Unifonic/Infobip keys as configured but only implements Taqnyat, so SMS can be enabled yet always return false. | `src/modules/sms/sms.service.ts:39–55` | Use explicit provider capability/config validation and truthful startup/readiness diagnostics. |
| F-454 | P1 | SMS transport has no visible timeout, retry/backoff, circuit breaker or provider status reconciliation; a transient API behavior can be collapsed into a boolean failure without delivery truth. | `src/modules/sms/sms.service.ts:44–60` | Define bounded transport/retry semantics and delivery-status lifecycle with observability. |
| F-455 | P1 | `sendOtp` accepts raw phone/OTP values with no visible normalization, country allowlist, abuse rate limit, message-template governance or replay binding in this layer. | `src/modules/sms/sms.service.ts:34–53` | Normalize/validate destinations, enforce per-identity/device/IP controls and secure template/OTP policies. |
| F-456 | P1 | Disabled SMS returns false and callers “fall back” externally, but this service does not communicate a typed reason or guarantee that fallback is configured, risking silent OTP delivery failure. | `src/modules/sms/sms.service.ts:6–16,34–42,57–60` | Return typed channel status and verify an available fallback before advertising OTP readiness. |
| F-457 | P1 | API-key-based provider calls have no visible secret-redaction/correlation policy and raw provider errors are reduced to stack logs, complicating incident response and leakage review. | `src/modules/sms/sms.service.ts:44–60` | Add safe structured telemetry, redaction and provider request correlation tests. |
| F-458 | P1 | The global SMS module exports a side-effecting transport service without visible lifecycle health, provider rotation or consent/opt-out policy. | `src/modules/sms/sms.module.ts:4–8; src/modules/sms/sms.service.ts:17–60` | Define channel governance, health checks, opt-out/consent and controlled provider rotation. |

## Mail findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-459 | P0 | OTP code is interpolated raw into HTML and plaintext without escaping or format validation, creating an injection/invalid-template risk if caller input is not already constrained. | `src/modules/mail/mail.module.ts:95–106` | Validate OTP format, escape all template values and contract-test malicious/invalid input. |
| F-460 | P1 | Recipient addresses and subjects are written into logs/events on failures and success, creating PII/metadata exposure without visible redaction, retention or access controls. | `src/modules/mail/mail.module.ts:77–90` | Use redacted structured telemetry with retention/access policy and leakage tests. |
| F-461 | P1 | Any Resend failure immediately triggers SES fallback with no idempotency or duplicate-delivery suppression, so a provider timeout after acceptance can produce two OTP/transactional emails. | `src/modules/mail/mail.module.ts:72–92` | Add message IDs/deduplication and provider reconciliation before fallback; test ambiguous timeouts. |
| F-462 | P1 | Resend/SES transport has no visible timeout, bounded retry/backoff, circuit breaker or delivery-status reconciliation, while `send` swallows exceptions into a boolean result. | `src/modules/mail/mail.module.ts:43–92` | Define bounded transport semantics, typed failure states and observable delivery lifecycle. |
| F-463 | P1 | `MAIL_FROM` and `SES_FROM` are runtime-configurable without visible domain allowlist, tenant authorization, DKIM/DMARC alignment or anti-spoof governance. | `src/modules/mail/mail.module.ts:35–37,63–65` | Enforce approved sender domains and startup/configuration validation. |
| F-464 | P1 | A new SMTP transporter is created for every SES send, increasing connection overhead and reducing control over pooling, lifecycle and backpressure under OTP/notification bursts. | `src/modules/mail/mail.module.ts:55–70` | Reuse a bounded transporter pool and load-test burst/failure behavior. |
| F-465 | P1 | OTP template is hard-coded Arabic/RTL and promises a ten-minute expiry without visible locale selection or binding to the actual OTP TTL/attempt policy. | `src/modules/mail/mail.module.ts:95–106` | Version localized templates and derive expiry text from the authoritative OTP contract. |

## Storage findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-466 | P0 | `StorageController` has no visible class-level JWT guard while normal and Cloudinary upload/read routes trust `@CurrentUser()`, creating an unauthenticated/null-user path risk that requires route registration verification. | `src/modules/storage/storage.module.ts:346–402` | Enforce explicit auth/role guards for private uploads and reads; test unauthenticated and cross-owner access. |
| F-467 | P0 | Public suggestion-image upload stores objects under `owner_account_id:'guest'` through the normal private R2 path, with no visible abuse quota, rate limit, moderation queue, orphan cleanup or content ownership lifecycle. | `src/modules/storage/storage.module.ts:376–395` | Isolate guest intake, apply quota/moderation/retention and test abuse/orphan cleanup. |
| F-468 | P1 | Upload validation checks declared MIME and approximate base64 length only; there is no visible magic-byte validation, image/PDF parser, virus scan implementation or decompression-bomb control. | `src/modules/storage/storage.module.ts:109–110,184–190,288–313,381–394` | Validate content bytes, scan/quarantine files and test polyglot/malformed/oversized inputs. |
| F-469 | P1 | Size policy is inconsistent: `MAX_BYTES` is 25 MB while the rejection text says 8 MB, and base64-length approximation is not an exact decoded-byte bound. | `src/modules/storage/storage.module.ts:109–110,184–187,289–291` | Define one authoritative byte limit and enforce decoded size exactly. |
| F-470 | P1 | Client-provided `original_name`, body MIME, owner_kind and Cloudinary custom key feed persistence/external object paths with limited normalization, enabling metadata injection, collisions or unsafe display names. | `src/modules/storage/storage.module.ts:184–216,302–313,346–358` | Normalize filenames/metadata, namespace keys robustly and test traversal/collision/display safety. |
| F-471 | P1 | `read`/`signedUrl` authorize only exact owner ID or role `'admin'`; organization, delegated-care, patient/provider relation and role taxonomy are not visible, while public_read objects bypass ownership entirely. | `src/modules/storage/storage.module.ts:219–275` | Define subject/resource scopes and public publication review; test all role/delegation cases. |
| F-472 | P1 | URL-driven deletion trusts prefix parsing and swallows all errors; database tombstoning and physical deletion can diverge without retry/outbox/reconciliation, leaving sensitive files or false deletion state. | `src/modules/storage/storage.module.ts:127–178` | Use durable deletion workflow, canonical object IDs and reconciliation/alerting tests. |
| F-473 | P1 | Object persistence stores inline base64 when S3 is not configured and logs a warning, creating a potentially large sensitive-data fallback with no visible encryption-at-rest, quota, retention or production fail-closed policy. | `src/modules/storage/storage.module.ts:112–125,203–215` | Disallow inline fallback in production, enforce encryption/quota/retention and test misconfiguration behavior. |
| F-474 | P1 | Uploads and object publication have no visible idempotency/checksum dedupe, per-owner quota, expiry job or audit trail; deterministic Cloudinary customKey overwrite can replace an existing asset without a revision/approval record. | `src/modules/storage/storage.module.ts:184–216,302–343` | Add lifecycle/idempotency/quota/audit and safe revisioned publication semantics. |

## Notification wrapper findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-475 | P1 | `NotificationService.create` forwards caller-controlled translation keys, params, type, priority and action with no local allowlist, schema validation or size bound, leaving injection/semantic-spoofing risk to downstream behavior. | `src/modules/notification/notification.service.ts:8–19` | Define typed notification DTOs, key/parameter allowlists and bounded payload tests. |
| F-476 | P1 | Optional caller-supplied `user_id` and `role` are accepted by the wrapper without visible authority checks, creating a confused-deputy risk if any caller can target another recipient or role. | `src/modules/notification/notification.service.ts:8–19` | Derive recipient from trusted actor/event context or enforce scoped producer permissions; test cross-user targeting. |
| F-477 | P1 | Authorization/ownership and read-state semantics are hidden behind a second service/module boundary, making it difficult to prove route-level contract coverage and increasing architectural drift/duplicate-service risk. | `src/modules/notification/notification.service.ts:4–31; src/modules/notification/notification.module.ts:5–9` | Establish one canonical notification service contract with explicit ownership/security tests and deprecate duplicate wrappers. |
| F-478 | P1 | The wrapper exposes mutation methods without visible idempotency, audit, rate-limit or delivery/channel semantics; duplicate creates and misleading read-state transitions remain possible unless downstream guarantees are universal. | `src/modules/notification/notification.service.ts:8–31` | Document/enforce replay/audit/rate/delivery contracts at the canonical boundary and test retries/outages. |

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-479 | P0 | Approval executes `RefundExecutor` before the return request is durably marked completed, with no visible transaction/outbox/idempotency/compensation; crash or retry can create financial/workflow divergence or duplicate refunds. | `src/modules/returns/returns.service.ts:156–185` | Use authoritative refund idempotency and durable saga state/reconciliation; test timeout/crash/replay. |
| F-480 | P0 | Admin decision accepts a body decision/note and relies on a role decorator plus exact runtime role, but the service lacks maker-checker, scope, version predicate and durable audit controls for irreversible refunds. | `src/modules/returns/returns.controller.ts:37–45; src/modules/returns/returns.service.ts:156–185` | Enforce scoped finance authorization, maker-checker/step-up, immutable audit and concurrency tests. |

| F-482 | P1 | Patient returns and admin returns list are unbounded; provider list first loads all provider order IDs before querying returns, creating data-exfiltration and scalability risk. | `src/modules/returns/returns.service.ts:123–147` | Add bounded pagination/projection, indexed provider query and load tests. |
| F-483 | P1 | Non-pharmacy return amount is resolved from a generic `orders` total without visible service-type/line-item/refundability validation, risking over-refund or incorrect commercial semantics. | `src/modules/returns/returns.service.ts:98–103` | Resolve canonical refundable amount per booking/service and test partial/cancelled/already-refunded states. |
| F-484 | P1 | Eligibility derives delivered age from `delivered_at || updatedAt || createdAt` and configurable window without explicit timezone/clock validity or policy version capture, making eligibility time-sensitive and non-reproducible. | `src/modules/returns/returns.service.ts:31–36,44–70` | Validate dates, version policy per request and test clock/timezone/config changes. |
| F-485 | P1 | Patient return creation has no visible idempotency key or duplicate-request guard, and admin processing only checks current status after a read, leaving replay/concurrent decision exposure. | `src/modules/returns/returns.controller.ts:11–14,37–45; src/modules/returns/returns.service.ts:73–120,156–185` | Add request/refund idempotency, atomic state predicates and replay/concurrency tests. |
| F-486 | P1 | `attachedDocs`, reason/details and admin note are accepted as raw values with no visible schema/size/content/ownership validation, potentially storing unsafe or unrelated documents/PII. | `src/modules/returns/returns.service.ts:73–120,156–163` | Use typed bounded DTOs, validate document ownership/type and apply retention/redaction policy. |

## Provider payout findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-487 | P0 | Payout routes have JWT protection but no visible provider-role, organization or account scope assertion; any authenticated identity reaching the route is treated as a provider ID for bank/balance operations. | `src/modules/payouts/provider-payouts.controller.ts:9–18,45–120` | Enforce provider-role/org scope and test patient/stranger/non-provider denial. |
| F-488 | P0 | Payout request and mine responses persist/return raw IBAN, bank name, bank account ID and balance snapshots without visible masking, field projection or finance-grade access policy. | `src/modules/payouts/provider-payouts.controller.ts:80–97,111–114` | Mask bank data, use strict DTOs and test logs/response/redaction leakage. |
| F-489 | P1 | Idempotency is supplied as a body field and a unique index is created lazily per request, creating contract drift and a race/deployment failure mode before the index exists. | `src/modules/payouts/provider-payouts.controller.ts:45–56` | Standardize `Idempotency-Key`, provision indexes at startup/migration and test concurrent first requests. |
| F-490 | P0 | Balance is calculated from mutable ledger entries and payout reservation is inserted with a locked ledger row, but correctness depends on ledger uniqueness/reconciliation outside this member; no visible invariant/negative-balance lock or version check protects all competing financial operations. | `src/modules/payouts/provider-payouts.controller.ts:27–42,62–97` | Enforce ledger invariants/unique reservation and cross-flow concurrency/reconciliation tests. |
| F-491 | P1 | The request stores `provider_type`, raw bank metadata and balance snapshots but has no visible currency/source-version or payout policy snapshot, making later dispute/replay semantics ambiguous. | `src/modules/payouts/provider-payouts.controller.ts:58–85` | Persist canonical currency/config/version and immutable policy evidence. |
| F-492 | P1 | No payout approval/settlement/rejection/retry/reconciliation route is visible in this controller, leaving the reservation lifecycle incomplete and requiring an explicit blocked-state contract. | `src/modules/payouts/provider-payouts.controller.ts:45–120` | Define the full state machine, failure compensation and operational reconciliation surface before enabling launch. |
| F-493 | P1 | Payout balance aggregation scans all provider ledger entries on every request and mine is only count-limited, creating performance/load risk and possible response overexposure. | `src/modules/payouts/provider-payouts.controller.ts:27–42,111–119` | Add indexed/materialized balance, bounded projections/pagination and load tests. |

## Legal Enterprise findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| F-496 | P1 | Acceptance snapshots and audit/commission logs persist user names, roles, IP, device, before/after objects and policy data without visible retention, minimization or field-level access policy. | `src/modules/legal/legal-enterprise.service.ts:26–48,112–147` | Define legal-record retention/access policy, minimize/redact telemetry and test PII boundaries. |
| F-497 | P1 | Settlement reports compute VAT using fixed 15% and commission from one config path, include broad orders/ledger data and return transfers, with no visible policy/version/currency reconciliation. | `src/modules/legal/legal-enterprise.service.ts:149–193` | Resolve canonical finance policy/version, reconcile to ledger and test formula/period/currency correctness. |
| F-498 | P1 | License cron auto-suspends providers and emits notifications in separate non-transactional operations; notification failures are swallowed and repeated/manual runs lack a durable idempotency/audit contract. | `src/modules/legal/legal-enterprise.service.ts:239–284` | Use idempotent audited suspension workflow, durable notification/outbox and failure/retry tests. |
| F-499 | P1 | Missing provider-insurance matrix defaults to accept-all and arbitrary company arrays are accepted, creating a fail-open insurance eligibility decision. | `src/modules/legal/legal-enterprise.service.ts:286–306` | Make acceptance policy explicit/verified, validate company IDs and test absent/stale matrix behavior. |
| F-500 | P1 | Provider settlement, insurance and SLA routes use JWT/user ID without visible provider-role/organization scope, risking cross-provider disclosure or mutation. | `src/modules/legal/legal-enterprise.controller.ts:75–123` | Enforce provider/org scope and owner/stranger/non-provider tests. |
| F-501 | P1 | SLA accepts unchecked `days`, loads all matching jobs, counts `sla_logs` without time window, and divides by `orders.length || 1`, producing potentially misleading rates and operational load. | `src/modules/legal/legal-enterprise.controller.ts:118–123; src/modules/legal/legal-enterprise.service.ts:308–363` | Validate bounded windows, align all source filters, aggregate/paginate and test zero/large periods. |
| F-502 | P1 | Consent mutation coerces any body value with `!!`, catches invalid-type errors into an HTTP-success-shaped response, and stores history without visible consent version/purpose/evidence binding. | `src/modules/legal/legal-enterprise.controller.ts:125–138; src/modules/legal/legal-enterprise.service.ts:365–387` | Use typed consent DTOs, proper 4xx errors, policy/version evidence and immutable audit tests. |

## Family and delegated-access findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-503 | P0 | Family invite codes use six-character `Math.random` values and are persisted without visible uniqueness, attempt throttling, redemption atomicity or brute-force monitoring. | `src/modules/family/family.service.ts:39–41,81–87,119–147` | Use cryptographic single-use invites, unique/indexed redemption, rate limits and abuse tests. |
| F-504 | P0 | `getMemberHealth` checks requester permission but does not verify that `targetMemberId` is a member of the same group before reading MedicalProfile, creating a delegated-access target validation gap. | `src/modules/family/family.service.ts:167–177` | Require target membership/relationship and test same-group, stranger and non-member IDs. |
| F-505 | P0 | Delegated records can expose health, prescriptions, lab/radiology status, appointments and emergency card fields based on broad permission keys, with no visible patient consent/expiry/revocation audit at read time. | `src/modules/family/family.service.ts:184–245` | Bind each scope to explicit consent, expiry/revocation and strict minimum-data policy. |
| F-506 | P1 | Underlying delegated-record query failures are converted to empty arrays/nulls, making infrastructure failure indistinguishable from a truthful empty health record. | `src/modules/family/family.service.ts:195–235` | Return typed partial/degraded status and telemetry instead of silent empty success. |
| F-507 | P1 | Permission requests accept arbitrary target member IDs, create duplicates without idempotency and respond without a pending-state predicate; separate grant update can race with status update. | `src/modules/family/family.service.ts:381–430` | Validate target membership, add idempotency/unique constraints and atomic pending transition/grant workflow. |
| F-508 | P1 | Member remove/leave/relation/permission/calendar mutations use read-then-update patterns without visible version predicates, idempotency or immutable audit, allowing stale/concurrent overwrites. | `src/modules/family/family.service.ts:150–159,248–279,282–290,363–376` | Add versioned atomic updates, audit and replay/concurrency tests. |
| F-509 | P1 | Emergency contacts expose other family members' phone numbers and names to every group member without visible per-contact consent, minimization or purpose restriction. | `src/modules/family/family.service.ts:293–309` | Require contact consent/purpose and redact/minimize fields by role. |
| F-510 | P1 | Calendar events are returned unbounded and include raw descriptions, references, colors and member labels; event creation lacks visible length/content bounds and ref ownership validation. | `src/modules/family/family.service.ts:314–360` | Bound/paginate and validate event payload/reference ownership; test cross-member disclosure. |
| F-511 | P1 | Duplicate legacy/contract routes expose the same member/removal capabilities with differing body names (`permissions` vs `scopes`, `members/:memberId` vs `remove-member/:userId`), increasing drift and inconsistent contract coverage. | `src/modules/family/family.controller.ts:47–85` | Consolidate/version one canonical contract and cover every retained alias identically. |

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-521 | P0 | Operations overview scans Redis presence/session keys and exposes counts based on key names; under load this can be expensive and may reveal session/presence metadata without a bounded indexed counter contract. | `src/modules/ops/ops.controller.ts:24–50` | Use maintained counters/observability aggregation, bound scans and test Redis load/failure. |
| F-522 | P1 | Admin activity and unified request feed expose actor IDs, patient/user/pharmacy IDs and human-readable names/summaries with no visible field-level minimization or purpose-specific access tiers. | `src/modules/ops/ops.controller.ts:96–115,123–135` | Redact/minimize identifiers and enforce least-privilege admin views. |
| F-523 | P1 | Overview aggregates all-time status groups across operational collections while daily/late metrics use inconsistent windows and failures silently become empty zeros, producing misleading dashboards. | `src/modules/ops/ops.controller.ts:72–94` | Define authoritative windows/status source, return degraded indicators and test partial outage. |
| F-524 | P1 | Request normalization hard-codes per-collection state names and uses Mongo `_id` as public ID although domain objects may use string `id`, risking broken links and status misclassification. | `src/modules/ops/ops.controller.ts:123–151` | Centralize domain state/ID adapters and contract-test every pipeline. |
| F-525 | P1 | `requests` fetches up to the requested limit from each collection before merging, so a limit of 100 can read 400+ records and still return a biased cross-domain slice. | `src/modules/ops/ops.controller.ts:118–158` | Use bounded indexed union/pagination with deterministic global ordering and load tests. |
| F-526 | P1 | Traffic accepts any regex-valid date despite documentation claiming up to 14 days back, enabling arbitrary historical metric access and potential expensive Redis reads. | `src/modules/ops/ops.controller.ts:161–173` | Enforce date window/role purpose and test old/future dates. |
| F-527 | P1 | All module setup installs `MetricsInterceptor` as `APP_INTERCEPTOR`, coupling this operational module to application-wide request instrumentation without visible sampling, privacy or failure-isolation policy. | `src/modules/ops/ops.module.ts:6–12` | Define global telemetry governance, redaction, sampling and fail-open/fail-closed behavior. |

## System health findings added during Phase 0B

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-528 | P1 | Liveness/readiness endpoints have no visible auth or perimeter annotation while revealing Mongo/Redis dependency status and process uptime, potentially exposing internal topology to unauthenticated callers. | `src/modules/system-health/system-health.controller.ts:5–37` | Define public/private probes, minimize details and test perimeter/rate policy. |
| F-529 | P0 | Readiness explicitly mirrors liveness, so a process can be reported ready using the same incomplete checks and cannot distinguish startup/operational dependency readiness from process health. | `src/modules/system-health/system-health.controller.ts:29–37` | Implement independent readiness contract with required dependencies and truthful HTTP statuses. |
| F-530 | P1 | `redisService.getClient().ping()` is called without visible null/error handling; Redis outage or uninitialized client can turn health probing into an exception rather than a controlled degraded result. | `src/modules/system-health/system-health.controller.ts:16–25` | Handle missing/failing dependencies deterministically and test outage/startup states. |
| F-531 | P1 | Every probe performs synchronous Mongo and Redis checks with no visible timeout, cache, rate limit or probe budget, allowing monitoring/public traffic to amplify dependency load. | `src/modules/system-health/system-health.controller.ts:13–26,29–36` | Bound checks, cache safely and test high-frequency/slow-dependency behavior. |
| F-532 | P1 | Health module imports the Redis module and exposes dependency check semantics without a separate internal observability boundary or versioned response contract. | `src/modules/system-health/system-health.module.ts:6–9; src/modules/system-health/system-health.controller.ts:13–37` | Version/document health schema and isolate internal diagnostics from public liveness. |

| ID | Severity | Finding | Direct evidence | Required acceptance condition |
